# QuillSign implementation plan

Tasks are recorded here and in Jira before implementation. Private deployment names, identifiers, domains, and personal configuration stay in ignored local files.

## Production self-hosting release gate

Owner scope confirmed on 2026-10-19: the reusable app must support self-hosting for real ordinary contracts. A successful demo/sandbox signing test does not complete this release gate. Production retention, deletion, legal holds, usable privileged operator controls and recovery acceptance are required release work; they are not deferred optional enhancements. This does not claim universal enforceability, regulated-use certification or qualified signatures.

Owner-authorized source sequence (2026-10-19): proceed independently with QUILS-146, QUILS-151, QUILS-152, QUILS-147, QUILS-148 and QUILS-149 while the separately pinned QUILS-139 sandbox validation runs. QUILS-150 still requires both the sandbox evidence and production lifecycle implementation. Existing demo policy and the approved bounded sandbox window remain unchanged. New cloud resources, permissions, live services and actual record destruction require a concrete separately approved rollout. AI placement remains deferred. No implementation story below is complete merely because this backlog is merged.

Status: Planning documentation deliverable; completion requires checked documentation PR merge and native Jira Done in the same pass. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Implementation remains in the following To Do stories.

The owner requires the reusable application to be ready for self-hosted real contracts, not merely the synthetic demo. Update the tracked implementation plan with production retention, deletion, privileged legal holds and self-hosted acceptance as required release work. Create separate dependent implementation stories with matching scope and measurable acceptance. This story delivers the backlog and planning documentation only, not the implementation or a readiness certification.

Treat a successful restricted demo sandbox run as evidence for its tested configuration, not completion of the production readiness gate. Preserve the existing demo policy and approved sandbox schedule/caps. Implementation follows in small dependent checked PRs after the current signing validation; keep future stories To Do until work starts. No cloud mutation, new service, live email/timestamp, permission change, actual record deletion or legal-hold operation is authorized by this planning task.

Acceptance: tracked plan and native Jira agree on the required production work, dependencies, limitations and release gates; distinguish configuration from missing implementation; preserve historical demo decisions; update ignored source-of-truth requirements with the owner decision. Validate documentation and privacy/secret checks; commit, push, create/attach and self-merge the small documentation PR after all exact-head checks pass. Move only this planning story to Done on merge; implementation stories remain open.

## QUILS-146: Define versioned production retention policy contracts

Status: Done after PR #152 passed all ten exact-head checks and merged; native Jira Done verified in the merge pass. Independent source-only prerequisite; production behavior remains unchanged.

Dependencies: QUILS-145. Owner explicitly authorized independent source implementation while the QUILS-139 sandbox test is pending on 2026-10-19. Remove the sandbox story as a source prerequisite; QUILS-150 retains it as a production acceptance gate. Do not change the pinned sandbox deployment or its approved package.

First small implementation slice of production retention: define immutable versioned policy/snapshot and state-clock decision contracts, explicit operator-owned finite completed retention or deliberately selected indefinite retention, finite abandoned-draft/expired/declined/voided rules and archive responsibility. Pending signing and incomplete completion produce no automatic deletion deadline until their authoritative lifecycle disposition exists. Missing/invalid policy must not select demo defaults. Persistence/audit integration remains required in QUILS-151, and disclosure/completion/access integration remains required in QUILS-152; none is dropped or declared complete by this slice.

Capture immutable policy values independently of later mutable options; validate bounded complete configuration, exact canonical snapshots/digests, lifecycle/clock boundaries, unsupported/unknown state, corrupted snapshots and overflow. Distinguish a retention eligibility date from authority to delete or proof of physical erasure. Ordinary indefinite retention is not a case-specific legal hold. Trusted state/time provenance must be supplied by later persistence integration; a digest alone grants no access or destructive capability.

Acceptance: meaningful pure configuration/canonicalization/state-table/clock/tamper/prospective-change tests, warning-free build, preserved existing behavior and permissive dependencies, small checked PR/secret scan/self-merge/native Done. Source-only contract with no active configuration binding, endpoints, database schema, retention change, deletion/hold command, cloud mutation, provider call or public email. The full production readiness gate remains open in the dependent stories.

Validation: all 37 focused production-policy cases pass with a warning-free Release build, covering every lifecycle clock, explicit indefinite retention, pending/incomplete disposition, prospective configuration, canonical replay/tamper/missing-field rejection and deadline overflow. No runtime registration, schema or hosted setting changed. Docker is unavailable locally; full existing SQL regression remains an exact-head CI gate.

## QUILS-151: Persist and audit frozen production retention policies

Status: Done after PR #153 passed all ten exact-head checks and merged; native Jira Done verified in the same pass. Required production implementation; the backend remains unregistered pending QUILS-152.

Dependencies: QUILS-146.

Implement the persistence part of the original QUILS-146 scope after its versioned policy contract merges. Freeze the correct non-demo record policy at creation/first enrollment, including explicit finite/indefinite settings, record origin and evidence-bound policy digest. Insert-only SQL/schema and append-only audit provenance must distinguish new enrollment from legacy/missing policy; no automatic backfill or reinterpretation of signed records. Capture terminal state/time anchors under the existing envelope transaction and recover uncertain commits idempotently. Settings changes apply prospectively; explicit extensions/migration preserve historical evidence. No demo enrollment changes, hosted migration/grant, live service or deletion. Test SQL provenance, concurrency, source/state bindings, tampering, rollback and restart/unknown commits. This work is a required production release prerequisite for legal holds and deletion, not optional deferred scope.

Creation enrollment is an explicit internal operation in the new envelope's caller-owned transaction; ordinary reads never enroll legacy records. Preserve the exact original creation timestamp and normalize the policy clock once to UTC seconds. Persist the canonical immutable policy plus enrollment audit binding atomically, and verify the existing saved evidence after uncertain commits without consulting current options. Terminal anchors bind one verified authoritative transition event and its exact original timestamp under the common envelope lock; expiry uses its effective deadline rather than delayed worker execution, and completion requires the actual publication evidence. Missing or inconsistent provenance grants no lifecycle or deletion authority. Keep the backend unregistered until QUILS-152 integrates it.

Validation: the warning-free Release build and all 44 local contract/guard regressions pass. Both full SQL integration CI runs pass after correcting the schema-count/append-only permission expectations and using an explicit production retention/protection fixture. Enrollment, provenance, runtime permissions, concurrency, rollback, lost acknowledgements and genuine terminal transitions are covered. All ten exact-head checks passed before merge. No local SQL success or hosted activation is claimed. Full enrolled admission-through-publication integration remains required in QUILS-152.

## QUILS-152: Bind production disclosures completion and access to frozen retention

Bind real-use disclosure/Send snapshots, final publication protection/deadlines and sender/signer/lookup/final reads to the exact saved production policy and terminal anchor. Preserve legacy publication recipe verification and all demo behavior. Never read mutable current options to shorten or extend an enrolled record, conflate ordinary indefinite retention with a case hold, rewrite signed evidence, restore expired sessions or authorize deletion. Pending signing and incomplete completion must remain explicit unresolved states rather than accidental purge candidates. Show actual retention/access and archive responsibility without hold reasons; fail closed for absent/inconsistent enrollment and insufficient storage protection. Validate meaningful SQL and real synthetic desktop/mobile/keyboard flows including clock boundaries, configuration changes, old recipes and corrupted evidence. Follow the local SEO regression runbook for visible routes. No fabricated operator policies, hosted settings/grants, live email/TSA or cloud policy changes. Replace ordinary admission's current fixed demo selection with explicit deployment-mode configuration. New production records require a complete production policy and truthful operator disclosures before admission and freeze their selected mode and policy at creation. Preserve historical demo records and restricted-pilot rules; changing the completed-retention setting alone must never silently convert existing records or make an installation production ready.

Implementation checkpoint: normal production admission/configuration preflight, exact sender review/Send disclosure hashing, frozen final-publication binding, atomic terminal anchors and common participant/worker retention reads are integrated in the draft branch. Final capabilities are bounded by the saved production access deadline, and saved enrollment/terminal prefixes remain verifiable after a large operational audit tail. Mode-aware footer and empty operator configuration examples are implemented. Warning-free Release build, 154 initial expanded pure/SQL/publication cases, 162 current configuration/Send/provenance/public-metadata cases, two fresh genuine production publication cases and 137 read-boundary/lost-acknowledgement regressions pass using disposable local SQL and Azurite. Full initial CI found seven upload lost-acknowledgement failures among 3380 cases: owned metadata-only reads committed before the actual upload. Those owned read snapshots now release by rollback, preserving caller-owned lifecycle commits; the original seven tests pass unchanged. Fresh full exact-head CI remains required.

Actual synthetic desktop/mobile/keyboard flows pass ordinary sender verification, PDF upload/confirmation, manual two-signer preparation, exact frozen production disclosure review/Send, both separate consent/adoption/application/exact-review/Finish sequences, verified current-copy progression and final publication. A fresh Windows checkout with merged QUILS-153 view mode completed the entire flow: both final PDFs render in separate Chrome tabs for the signer and the completion-only recipient, including keyboard activation, without a save prompt. The recipient redeemed its captured completion link into a separate limited readonly session. Independent disposable SQL verifies two signed/processed checkpoints, one enrollment/terminal anchor, stored UTC offsets and an exact 365-day interval from actual completion. Four local completion messages contain both attachment metadata and the saved deadline, with no demo wording. The earlier saved executed download also matched its original published SHA-256. Browser native filesystem saving of this new inline pair and real hosted delivery are not claimed. No hosted settings, services, grants, deployment or live email/TSA/conversion changed. Production legal holds/deletion/hosted acceptance remain dependent work.
Bind explicit Records configuration with complete operator-policy preflight for new production admission. Keep all private configuration examples empty. A production deployment must show mode-appropriate footer text rather than the synthetic-demo warning, while the demo retains its existing warning; follow public metadata/noindex/auth/cache regressions and actual desktop/mobile/keyboard checks. Document that these runtime controls do not complete pending legal-hold/deletion or hosted acceptance work.

Preserve completed access after a valid operational audit tail grows beyond the original bounded signing snapshot. Enrollment verification authenticates its exact creation prefix; terminal verification authenticates the frozen transition/anchor prefix and still rejects duplicate or orphaned retention/terminal events. Later legitimate download/access events must neither renew a deadline nor make the saved production policy unreadable. Keep all prefix reads bounded and existing publication recipes unchanged.

Windows checkout correction: canonicalize generated retention notice newlines before disclosure hashing/verification. A CRLF source checkout previously caused ordinary production admission to fail closed. The warning-free CRLF build and all 41 focused contracts pass; Windows/Linux CI now verifies the production admission contracts, with explicit CRLF source on Windows. A fresh genuine production browser flow passes with this fix and the merged optional view setting. Final combined exact-head CI remains required before merge.

## QUILS-147: Preserve production records with authorized durable legal holds

Status: In Progress. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. QUILS-146/151/152 merged and native Done. Required for production self-hosting readiness; no hosted activation claimed.

Dependencies: QUILS-146, QUILS-151, QUILS-152.

Implement durable case-scoped legal-hold commands and evidence on the production lifecycle policy foundation. Select and document an explicit operator authentication/authorization mechanism appropriate to the existing no-account participant model before implementing privileged endpoints. Ordinary sender/signer magic links must never grant hold placement, release or arbitrary archive access. Require attributable operator identity, purpose/reason and reference, exact record scope, command idempotency, protected transport, CSRF where applicable, and append-only placement/release evidence with restricted sensitive details.

Preserve the complete defined evidence bundle under an active hold: original/normalized/current/final PDFs, signatures and referenced assets, disclosure snapshots, audit/acceptance/publication evidence and necessary contact/history data. Coordinate authoritative hold state with database cleanup, worker leases, object versions, storage recovery markers and provider-level protection. Fence hold placement/release against concurrent purge decisions and individual destructive operations; a hold cannot be reported effective for already deleted evidence. Reconcile uncertain commits, protection failures and partial deletion without silently clearing a hold or claiming preservation that did not occur.

Release is a separate explicit privileged, reviewed and audited command. Multiple active holds require release of every relevant hold before deletion eligibility. Releasing a hold must not shorten locked storage retention, restore expired participant sessions or grant new public access; reevaluate the original retention policy without resetting its clock. Failure or inconsistent hold/protection state blocks deletion and is visible to operators. Acceptance includes unauthorized/replay/CSRF rejection, concurrent placement-versus-purge, multiple holds, restart/recovery, partial deletion and release-versus-retention cases. Actual provider permissions/policy mutations remain separately reviewed rollout work.

Operator mechanism selected before privileged endpoint implementation: separate, default-disabled OpenID Connect operator sign-in and dedicated short-lived protected cookie, exact configured issuer/subject grants, and separate status/hold-place/hold-release/evidence-read permissions. Participant and pilot sessions never qualify. The initial internal contract checkpoint bounds verified ticket authority and exact record/command/revision confirmations; it registers no handler, route or runtime hold service. Durable bundle preservation, provider protection, deletion fencing and the full concurrency/recovery acceptance remain mandatory before this story is complete. QUILS-148 supplies the private operator browser workflow; configuring a live identity provider remains separate rollout work.

Initial local validation covers twelve pure authorization/command cases: participant/pilot/wrong-scheme denial, exact issuer/subject and duplicate-claim checks, distinct permissions, current-grant revocation, ticket lifetime and expiry, exact command/record/hold/revision/private-detail digest bindings, malformed Unicode and overflow rejection. No database hold or provider protection is implemented by this checkpoint; keep the story In Progress and its PR draft while the durable backend is built.

Next internal backend checkpoint: persist append-only private pending hold commands under the common production record lock, binding exact policy, operator, command and global record revision with authenticated encrypted details. Deny ordinary runtime/participant SQL access. Pending placement and pending release both block deletion; neither implies effective provider preservation or completed release. Cover identical replay versus changed-command conflicts, multiple independent holds, rollback/unknown commits, restart/tampering, bounded reads, authority expiry/revocation and concurrent revision races. Keep the backend unregistered and privileged routes absent until full inventory/protection/deletion coordination is implemented.

Pending-command ledger implemented with one private append-only table and protected per-record evidence. All 51 focused cases pass with a warning-free build, including fresh SQL migrations/model match, runtime/worker denial, both same-record revision and cross-record command-ID races, rollback, lost commit acknowledgement/replay, encrypted operator details, corrupted evidence and multiple pending holds/releases. No runtime SQL grant or handler was added. Provider inventory/preservation receipts, mutation/deletion fencing integration, actual effective/released states and the remaining story acceptance are still incomplete; keep PR #156 draft and Jira In Progress.

Inventory step: a bounded exact-record SQL snapshot under the verified pending-hold revision/fence and common parent transaction, with explicit model coverage, row/byte preflight and range locks, canonical scalar/schema/content digests and authenticated encrypted output. Preserve owned contact/history/acceptance/publication/work/token evidence and only lookup retention tied to the owned outbox; never include unrelated global lookup, pilot or suppression data. Reject unexpected pilot association, stale fences, unknown schema and over-limit snapshots. This internal unregistered snapshot grants no participant access and is only one component of the future complete blob/provider preservation proof.

SQL inventory component implemented: all 59 combined focused cases pass warning-free, including eight inventory cases for exact ownership/global exclusion, capture-time-independent content fingerprints, changed evidence, bound encrypted payloads, stale fence rejection, row/database/serialized-byte limits, unexpected pilot association and an independent writer blocked by actual SQL range locks. Only encrypted snapshot output is returned; no source rows or participant authority are changed. Referenced-object inventory, provider protection and recovery receipts, mutation/deletion coordination and effective/released states remain required; this does not complete QUILS-147.

## QUILS-148: Add private production retention and legal-hold operator controls

Status: To Do. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Required for production self-hosting readiness; no implementation or hosted activation claimed.

Dependencies: QUILS-146, QUILS-147.

Build the minimal privileged self-hosting operator workflow on the production policy and hold command foundations. Use their explicit operator authentication/authorization boundary; do not create a general document-browsing privilege for participant links or expose a public administrator signup. Provide bounded exact-record search/status, policy/deadline and preservation/deletion state, an evidence inventory/export for an authorized purpose, deliberate hold placement and separately reviewed release, and clear acknowledgement of incomplete/uncertain outcomes.

Display ordinary retention, blocked deletion, active holds and actual storage protection accurately. Separate permission to administer a hold from authority to read sensitive document contents. Restrict operator identity/reasons/references and exports; use clean URLs, no-store/noindex/no-referrer responses, request bounds, rate limits, CSRF, expiring purpose-bound confirmations and immutable audit. No raw provider IDs/capabilities or actions in query strings, no implicit action on GET, bulk destructive defaults or record/session resurrection.

Acceptance: real synthetic desktop/mobile/keyboard flows cover authorized status, export, hold, release and missing/wrong/expired privilege, stale state, replay, concurrency and unknown outcomes; unauthenticated/default-disabled routes expose no record metadata. Follow the local page/metadata regression runbook when implementing visible routes. This is a required production operator capability, not a demo participant feature. Keep deployment private settings and operator access configuration out of Git.

## QUILS-149: Delete expired production records with hold-aware durable recovery

Status: To Do. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Required for production self-hosting readiness; no implementation or hosted activation claimed.

Dependencies: QUILS-146, QUILS-147, QUILS-148.

Extend durable lifecycle maintenance to ordinary production records after the policy, legal-hold foundation and usable privileged operator controls are ready. Define a complete owned inventory and state-specific eligibility for expired drafts, declined/voided/expired requests and completed records. Verify the saved policy, active holds and actual immutable version protection before scheduling and immediately around each destructive boundary. Preserve completed records until their retained-copy and archive commitments allow deletion; email submission alone is not proof of those commitments.

Use exact opaque record/version references, conditional deletion, bounded batches/cursors, durable idempotent intents, leases/fencing and storage-only recovery rather than recurring idle SQL polling. Cover replaced/orphan assets, checkpoints, final versions, signature assets, disclosures, protected outbox/token payloads, contact/lookup records and audit data according to the reviewed retention/hold rules. Explicitly decide what minimal non-identifying tombstone is retained to reconcile late writes and prevent restoration/replay; do not erase the only authority needed for safe recovery before storage cleanup is verified.

Active holds and unexpired or inconsistent protection block deletion; the worker must never shorten/remove locked retention or release holds to make progress. Reconcile SQL/Blob partial failure, cancellation, crash/restart, duplicate events, uncertain provider outcomes and late writes without exceeding scope or restoring participant access. Report logical expiry, inaccessible, pending deletion, protection/hold blocked and verified active-data cleanup separately. Soft-delete, historical versions, backups, logs and participant-held copies have explicit documented limits; do not claim universal physical erasure.

Acceptance: accelerated synthetic clock plus real bounded integration/browser/operator flows demonstrate finite and indefinite policies, all terminal states, concurrent hold/release races, protected versions, retries and late-write recovery. Existing seven-day demo semantics and failure-safe cleanup remain unchanged. No actual production/cloud deletion or permission/policy rollout in source implementation without a reviewed operator-approved package.

## QUILS-150: Validate production lifecycle recovery and self-hosting release readiness

Status: To Do. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Required for production self-hosting readiness; no implementation or hosted activation claimed.

Do not reuse the synthetic disclosure profile or describe a purchased certificate as sufficient legal readiness.

Define and exercise backup/restore and key/certificate renewal/recovery with preserved historic verification and legal holds. A restored database, storage recovery marker or older backup must not silently bypass a later hold, resurrect purged public access, rerun old mail or delete based on stale policy; quarantine or explicitly reconcile inconsistent restore state before maintenance resumes. Document backup/soft-delete/log retention and legal-hold responsibilities, privacy/data-request handling, outage/retry/ambiguous-delivery procedures and monitoring without private deployment inventories. Clarify any manual operator steps and remaining unsupported capabilities rather than claiming automatic protection.

Acceptance: synthetic non-demo deployment checks the full sender-to-two-signers-to-retainable-final-packet flow and exact evidence exports, actual policy-based expiry/purge, hold blocks, audited release after policy expiry, interrupted cleanup, restart, backup restoration and key/certificate transition. Complete a scoped production security/access review and the applicable existing e-signature acceptance checklist; keep ordinary-signature, mailbox-authentication and specialized-use limits explicit. A sandbox pass alone cannot close this release gate. Any hosted resource, grant, email or paid-service test requires its own concrete scoped owner approval; local fake/captured tests need no live services. AI placement remains deferred.

## QUILS-101: Add sender invitation resend UI and delivery status

Status: Done on merge after current-head checks pass. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Local captured email only; depends on merged QUILS-93.

Add the verified-sender invitation resend review/confirmation UI and status integration on merged QUILS-93. This is a separate sender-facing slice using the existing internal admission/delivery service. Inspect the current sender status/authentication/notice and resend evidence contracts after this story and matching docs/plan.md entry exist; refine both together before implementing any design change. No reminders, contact correction, new mail transport, hosted enablement or operator-cap defaults.

Only the authenticated sender for a live retained Sent envelope may review and explicitly request a same-address replacement for the current unfinished sequential signer. Bind an expiring purpose-separated protected review to the original protected sender-ticket expiry, exact current signer/PDF/disclosure/envelope revision and server-generated idempotency GUID. Enforce CSRF, existing entry rate limits and current gates; never trust client-provided authority, IDs or expiry. Use the fixed resend affirmation and describe older unused link invalidation, preserved redeemed sessions/consent and queued-versus-delivered meaning accurately. Reuse durable admission and ambiguity handling; retries/refreshes must not create duplicate mail or bypass pending/uncertain prior delivery. Do not expose raw tokens, provider IDs or internal work IDs.

Integrate accurate latest original/replacement invitation delivery state into sender status, distinguishing queued, provider accepted, definite failure, feedback and uncertain submission without claiming inbox delivery or enabling blind retry. Preserve original Send/current-copy/signing/Finish/expiry/void/retention evidence and sequential scope; handle source/auth/retention changes and status reads safely. Keep bounded database reads, event-driven work and SQL auto-pause. Preserve existing disabled-by-default resend/email/work settings and explicit caps; local synthetic hosts may enable only their own fake/Mailpit configuration.

Use clean redirects and protected short-lived HttpOnly one-use notices (JSON clients receive JSON), private cache/indexing/referrer/CSP headers and no analytics or private metadata exposure. Sender routes remain excluded from indexable metadata and sitemap; follow PLAN.SEO.md regression scope for route/page changes. No shared public branding/layout or unrelated page-content change. Exercise the complete local sender review/confirmation/status/replacement-link flow on desktop, mobile and keyboard, including scanner-safe GET, CSRF, authority, stale/tampered/expired review, replay/concurrency, disabled/capped/suppressed/pending/uncertain outcomes and preserved signer session/consent/Finish. Run appropriate full SQL/Azurite, browser, model/vendor/license, secret and exact-head CI checks; commit/push/open/attach/merge PR and native Jira Done. Email testing remains fake/captured or loopback Mailpit only; no live email/TSA, cloud/resource/configuration/deployment/permission change, purchase, dependency substitution or AI.

Concrete design: add private /draft/{publicId}/resend GET and CSRF-protected POST with no client script. A purpose-separated encrypted review proof binds sender identity/scope, original authentication-ticket expiry, exact QUILS-93 review, server-generated stable command GUID and display title. Expire it after at most ten minutes, the sender ticket or the original UTC day, whichever is first. Never accept form-supplied IDs, addresses, source hashes or expiry as authority. Check the separate trusted review deadline at entry and immediately before admission commit, including rollback if SQL wakeup or audit work crosses it; retain the original sender-ticket expiry as its own authority binding. GET/previews are metadata-only and report pending/uncertain, suppression and retention unavailability before offering confirmation; caps remain authoritative at admission. Preserve the same proof on failed/unknown confirmation and require a fresh affirmation; check saved status without re-enqueueing. A new explicit review is a new deliberate action, still subject to existing caps and predecessor checks.

Read latest replacement delivery from the canonical admission and protected outbox/work scope per signer under the status envelope transaction, with bounded reads. Before status/review/admission and obsolete-original dispatch, verify envelope-local admission/outbox index cardinality and structural signer/recipient/ID bindings using bounded scalar SQL results; malformed, missing or inconsistent admissions must not silently disappear from latest-invitation selection. Keep signing progress and latest invitation delivery as separate values so existing consent/session state never makes a replacement look opened or delivered. Show queued, provider acceptance, feedback, definite failure, uncertain submission and expired dispatch truthfully, with recorded admission time and no provider/work/token identifiers. Do not infer a new link was opened from an older session. Keep the original invitation path compatible, and fail closed on unverifiable replacement evidence. Add explicit no-store/no-referrer/noindex headers to the changed sender routes including rejected responses; keep them untracked and outside public metadata/discovery. Reuse existing sender rate policy settings with separate resend read/action partitions, protected one-use notices and clean status redirects. No polling or new operator limits/defaults. Local browser proof uses a disposable synthetic sender host, fake/captured transport or loopback Mailpit; production settings remain unchanged.

Validation: the final 170-case sender/resend/Send/void/analytics and genuine enrolled-retention HTTP run passes, including confirmation expiry crossed during SQL admission with rollback and malformed/missing admission index keys that must not fall back to original-invitation success. Browser Chrome desktop/390-pixel mobile/keyboard checks exercise unchecked rejection, explicit confirmation, clean redirect and one-use notice, queued-to-submitted local Mailpit status, old-link rejection, captured replacement redemption and both PDF pages; signing progress remains separate from delivery state. No horizontal overflow, private analytics or console warnings/errors were observed. Release builds, unchanged EF model, 20 Node tests, pinned vendor/license checks, documentation links and history secret scan pass. Full local regression and exact-head CI gate merge. No hosted setting/deployment or live email/TSA call.

## QUILS-97: Prepare isolated hosted DOCX conversion enablement

Status: Done. Setup proposal merged as PR #99. The owner subsequently approved the bounded pilot, tracked separately by QUILS-100. Owned by this chat; separate from the reminders/resend/contact-correction/Office-format chat.

Owner asked what is needed to enable hosted DOCX conversion. Existing package gate, conversion client, persistence and browser workflow are implemented and locally validated; identify and prepare the smallest concrete hosted Gotenberg isolation, connectivity and validation package. Assess current dedicated project environment and source after this story and docs/plan.md exist. Require private web-to-converter reachability, bounded request/response, safe Gotenberg configuration and demonstrated denial of outbound document-driven network requests; a URL filter is not a substitute for network isolation. Compare practical Azure approaches with current official documentation and low-volume costs, preserve Consumption/scale-to-zero and avoid assumed NAT/Firewall/private endpoint approvals. Deliver a concrete proposed Bicep/configuration/resource/permission delta and private operator instructions for owner approval before any cloud change or enablement. Keep hosted email and conversion disabled until approved rollout/testing; no live email, timestamp calls, purchases, DNS, AI, unrelated resources or additional Office formats in this story. Parent owns DOCX infrastructure; independent Office workflow chat owns additional format source work. Validate proposal/source claims, preserve secrets, small PR/checks/self-merge and native Jira Done. Findings: current hosting uses Consumption without a custom virtual network. Ordinary private ingress and Gotenberg URL rules do not provide the required general outbound isolation. The subscription advertises Azure Container Apps SandboxGroups and its group-scoped Data Owner role in the current deployment region. Prepare a bounded feasibility pilot proposal using one SandboxGroup, maximum one sandbox, 1 vCPU/2 GiB, a 180-second operator-enforced execution deadline with separate inactivity auto-suspend, the pinned existing Gotenberg image, deny-all/full-inspection egress, authenticated file/exec/download APIs and no public converter port. The existing web identity would receive the sandbox data role scoped only to that new group; pass no storage/SQL/email/provider credentials into the sandbox. Use fresh per-conversion sandbox state, no persistent user volumes/snapshots, immediate deletion and cleanup verification. This is a proposal, not authorization or a claim that the provider integration works. Limit an approval request to two synthetic conversions and a total USD 1 pilot budget, preserving email and public converter enablement gates. Current retail compute rates imply about USD 0.08-0.24/month for 44 conversions lasting 60-180 seconds at that size, before shared grants; converted-image storage size, future storage billing, data/logging and actual lifecycle cost must be measured before any under-USD-1 recurring-cost claim. A private Bicep draft and exact pilot scope/cost/cleanup instructions make the proposed change reviewable; deploy no resource/role or provider call before approval. Approval package refinement: add temporary SandboxGroup Data Owner access for the already-signed-in operator on this one new group and remove that role after the pilot, in addition to the proposed existing web-identity group-scoped role. No subscription-wide grant or new identity. Use the documented GA ARM 2026-07-01 shape; CPU/count/time limits belong to the data-plane operator plan rather than unsupported ARM defaults. The inspected Microsoft MIT Python SDK 0.1.0b4 is a community-preview operator aid, not a new application dependency. Turn off SDK write retries; record exact run labels/IDs before continuing, reconcile uncertain creates without blind repetition, inspect exact cleanup and preserve unrelated resources. Stop/suspend can retain snapshots and is not deletion. ## QUILS-93: Design safe invitation replacement and implement bounded resend admission

Status: Done on merge after current-head checks pass. Assigned to ChatGPT Codex. Internal admission/delivery only; source-only and local captured email.

Concrete QUILS-93 slice: implement internal verified-sender review/admission and supported durable replacement-invitation delivery; no public route, form or automatic reminder. Require the original protected sender-ticket expiry, exact reviewed current signer/PDF/disclosure/envelope revision, an explicit fixed resend statement and a server-chosen idempotency GUID. Same-address resend expires older unused SignerAccess tokens atomically with admission; it preserves used tokens, already-redeemed sessions, consent, adopted marks, fields, accepted Finish and immutable Sent/checkpoint evidence. Refuse non-current or finished signers and terminal/expired/retention-cutoff requests.

Reuse envelope-owned EmailOutbox/WorkItems, protected purpose-separated resend payloads, hash-chained canonical admission audit and storage hints; no schema, grants, resources or new persistent store. Envelope-first locks serialize authority, token revocation and original/replacement dispatch. A transaction application lock serializes daily admission reservations. Explicitly configured per-address/IP/sender/global caps are required before enabling the internal service; default Enabled=false and empty cap values impose no new hosted/operator policy. Count every admitted resend, including queued/failed requests, in bounded protected outbox reads (global hard maximum 1000); never count a retry as a new admission. Clip dispatch to its original UTC day and existing signing/retention limits. Refuse admission if immutable document retention can purge its cap reservation before that UTC day ends; do not extend retention or silently weaken caps.

Link each resend to the exact latest invitation/resend and its canonical audit. Refuse live/queued prior work and uncertain submission/receipt state; only known terminal delivery or definite non-submission can be replaced. Fence old original and replacement work against the saved latest resend so later trusted replay cannot issue obsolete links. Recheck current source, sequential eligibility, retention, enablement, suppression, configured caps, exact work lease and protected intent at dispatch. Tokens activate only at dispatch, bounded by existing invitation/session and signing/retention settings. Definite non-submission invalidates only the exact unused issued token and clears only its exact owned intent for bounded retry. Unknown SQL/provider/receipt outcomes retain intent and use existing no-blind-retry recovery. Record provider acceptance truthfully without asserting delivery, preserve late feedback and prevent stale provider returns from regressing signing state or recreating purged rows.

Test actual local SQL/Azurite with captured/fake mail and at least one loopback SMTP Mailpit replacement-link redemption. Cover authority/review/CSRF-caller contract, sequential and Finish races, old link rejection with active-session/consent preservation, source/canonical-payload/lease tampering, UTC-day caps and cutoff/config changes, disabled/suppressed behavior, duplicate/concurrent requests, original-work fencing, rollback, lost hints/commit receipts, definite versus unknown transport and receipt recovery. Sender UI/status integration is a later independent slice; existing original-invitation status remains unchanged here. No live mail/provider/TSA, hosted configuration/deployment/cloud/permission change, AI or new operator policy. It requires an explicitly suitable configured policy; no silent extension or claim of earlier enrollment.

Cleanup uses a static same-owner SQL stored procedure with worker-only EXECUTE, no dynamic SQL/EXECUTE AS or general document/blob authority. Concrete design: an internal idempotent request takes a server-chosen GUID, normalized single mailbox and canonical IP. Disabled gates precede SQL/signals. A shared transaction application lock protects configurable daily per-address/IP/global admission caps (defaults 3/10/50; global hard bound 1000), suppression and exact existing-ID scope. Queue envelope-null EmailOutbox/WorkItem rows with technical work metadata only. A dedicated protected mail payload binds request ID, mailbox, private IP hash, original request time and fixed dispatch deadline (default/max 24 hours). Persist the storage hint before SQL commit and publish after confirmed commit. Unknown mailboxes use the same path without document-membership queries; return no link or work ID.

Dispatch through a lazy lookup delegate in EmailWorkHandler using the existing Email kind, WorkStore and WorkProcessor. Verify the current SQL lease and exact protected outbox scope before calling DocumentLookup.IssueAsync in its existing standalone transaction at dispatch. Unexposed unused issuance after failed preparation is technical state, never returned authority. Render a private fragment link/manual code through new Razor views, with absolute UTC expiry, no attachments/tracking and existing demo/retention warnings.

Before submission, reacquire the application lock and work/mail/token locks in a consistent order; recheck enabled/suppression policy, request deadline, current-day delivery caps, exact unused token revision/hash and lease. Persist protected technical issuance binding plus submission intent and commit before provider I/O; recheck time/gates afterward. Record a provider receipt only against that exact intent, preserving feedback status. Definite non-submission clears only the owned exact intent and invalidates its unconsumed exact token before retry. Unknown commit/provider acknowledgement or lost authority leaves intent for existing ambiguous recovery, without blind provider or consumed-token replay. Reuse schema, lazy credentials and event-driven recovery. Most tests use lightweight empty-mailbox fixtures, plus one genuine completed-packet captured-mail redemption flow. ## QUILS-86: Add private completed-document lookup browser flow

Status: Done (PR #91). All 12 applicable exact-head checks passed with 1,880 .NET and 15 Node tests; native Jira Done verified after merge. Depends on merged QUILS-84 (PR #89) and QUILS-85 (PR #90). Owned by this chat.

Depends on merged QUILS-84 (PR #89) and QUILS-85 (PR #90). Add the private browser flow needed before lookup mail can target a working destination: scanner-safe /find/access entry, explicit CSRF/rate-limited single-use code redemption, a dedicated short-lived lookup principal/private HttpOnly receipt and a private completed-document list. Capture and immediately scrub an emailed fragment in the browser; never redeem from GET or place private proofs in query strings, logs or later-page HTML. Keep a manual-code path.

Show only the authenticated mailbox's retained verified completed records at its original snapshot ceiling, with bounded paging whose protected cursor stays in cookies or POST bodies. Explicit CSRF-protected Open uses the QUILS-85 handoff and signs into the existing narrow completed-record reader before a clean confirmation redirect. Preserve original lookup/reader/retention bounds, every frozen party role, nonce/purpose isolation, privacy headers, clean one-use notices and safe storage/source/expiry recovery. Use actual completed non-agreement fixtures and captured local test support only. No live email enablement, actual TSA request, Azure/AWS mutation, schema/dependency change, purchase or AI. Commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done.

Concrete browser design: use /find/access/{handler?} for a two-phase entry. A fragment is scrubbed before any request and kept only in a short-lived JavaScript closure until the user chooses Continue. Begin POST validates the protected lookup transport and stores it in a dedicated HttpOnly pending cookie, with a fresh nonce and at most ten minutes bounded by the link expiry. A separate explicit CSRF-protected Redeem POST consumes the code once and issues the named lookup cookie ticket plus private receipt; no GET or automatic script redeems it. Manual codes use the same two phases. Private data stays out of query strings and subsequent HTML.

Use /find/documents/{handler?} for the private list, Next/First paging and Open. Authenticate only the named lookup principal with its matching receipt. Keep the protected session-bound keyset cursor in an HttpOnly paging cookie updated by CSRF-protected POSTs, then redirect to the clean list URL. Listing/reload is read-only and never refreshes original authority. Open calls LookupPacketAccess, checks remaining original browser/session time, signs into CompletionReaderBrowser and redirects to its clean exact-packet page; existing final download controls remain responsible for explicit preparation and exact-file validation. Add path-wide no-store/no-referrer headers and bounded IP entry/read/action policies, with clean recovery notices. Reuse existing branding/layout and source fonts/assets; no new dependency, mail handler or public email-request form. Delivery follows only after this destination is supported.

Actual loopback browser flows passed with genuine completed synthetic non-agreement packets and a captured local TSA: scanner-safe fragment/manual entry, keyboard Continue/confirmation, private two-document paging/reset, explicit handoff, successful executed/certificate downloads, mobile layout, missing-storage failure and explicit recovery, cross-tab stale nonce rejection without losing the newer link, consumed-link rejection, empty lookup, original-session expiry with a clean one-use notice and expired-link failure. Temporary host stopped and viewport restored. No cloud mutation, live mail or real TSA request in this slice. Full existing coverage and container checks remain required on the exact PR head before merge.

Concrete handoff design: authenticate the dedicated lookup principal/private receipt before selecting a matching completed candidate, bounded by its original completion-time snapshot. Verify the actual current immutable final pair outside the write transaction. Then take the common envelope lock followed by an update/hold lock on the consumed lookup token; recheck the original revision, nonce, expiry, frozen source and saved first-upload deadline. Choose matching frozen sender first, then signer by order, then completion recipient. A cursor or bare email never authorizes a handoff.

Factor the existing completion-access locked consumption primitive so normal emailed-link redemption retains its canonical issuance/consumption and once-only behavior. Within the same caller-owned transaction, issue and immediately consume a fresh exact-party/publication completion token and append a canonical lookup-handoff audit tying technical lookup and reader identifiers to the chosen publication/party. No raw final link leaves memory. Clip the new readonly reader's original expiry to the earliest of its normal configured limit, the original lookup session expiry and current retention; do not reset that bound on retries. Reread the exact saved upload policy before commit. Recheck lookup/source/retention before commit, then authenticate the committed exact reader, original lookup and current retention again before returning its receipt. Unknown acknowledgements return no receipt; a later explicit still-authenticated lookup operation may safely create a fresh bounded reader without replaying the prior consumed transport. Original mailed completion links keep their existing independent lifetime. No schema, cloud, mail, public endpoint or actual timestamp call.

Status: Done on merge. Internal source foundation; public lookup remains a later slice. Depends on merged completion access, publication and retention work. Owned by this chat.

Verify mailbox ownership through a dedicated, purpose-bound single-use lookup token and short-lived read-only identity; persist only token hashes and keep capabilities private. Inspect existing token/completion access and purge boundaries after matching plan creation and refine this story/plan before implementation as needed. Lookup design refinement (QUILS-84): Concrete design: reuse the existing Lookup purpose with envelope/party/document/publication fields absent. Store only the random 256-bit token hash and technical lifecycle fields; keep the mailbox solely inside protected link/session payloads, so lookup adds no orphaned mailbox data beyond envelope purge. The protected issuance transport binds normalized mailbox, token/hash, issuance time, original expiry and issuance row revision. Any write to the token invalidates the original link, including a reset of UsedAt after consumption. Redeem once under the token lock and return a separate nonce/revision-bound read-only receipt after confirmed commit. Reject legacy bare hashes and cross-purpose/mixed principals. Unknown commit returns no transport/session; an explicitly issued fresh link is the recovery path.

Use bounded 15-minute link and 30-minute session defaults (maximum 60 minutes each), an independent original session expiry, and protected session-bound keyset cursors. Each finite page considers at most five candidates by default (maximum ten), with a completion-time snapshot ceiling. Verify each current publication/frozen party and actual final pair using existing completion verification, recheck saved first-upload retention and current source after I/O, and authenticate again before returning minimal title/public ID/completion/retention metadata. Deduplicate envelopes with overlapping party roles. Listing does not issue final-access links or create download/edit authority. No schema change is needed; extend the existing token EF mutation guard to Lookup. Validate SQL translation, pagination and genuine published evidence locally. Public delivery/rate limiting and UI remain dependent work.

Validation: the initial 29 SQL/Azurite cases passed, including genuine multi-publication pagination, every frozen party role, corrupted/missing storage, expiry, concurrent redemption and failed commits. Three final-revision regressions passed after issuance-revision binding and fixture consolidation, covering reset/rewrite rejection, unpublished/later-completed snapshots and rollback/unknown-commit recovery. Release builds, 11 Node cases, pinned PDF.js/interact.js/signature_pad checks, the EF pending-model check and staged/full-history secret scans passed. Exact-head full CI is required before merging. No public UI, cloud change, live timestamp call or email enablement in this slice.

Give each synthetic seal fixture a distinct root subject, preserving strict production chain verification, configured trust, signature and timestamp checks. Reproduce the affected lookup and seal checks on Linux and Windows and require a fresh exact-head full CI pass. No live TSA or provider-policy change.

Represent relationships, case-insensitive party email uniqueness, unique token hashes and audit sequences, normalized field bounds, concurrency controls, append-only hash-chain support, and lease/retry metadata. Apply migrations to a fresh local database and document the separate migration command; do not auto-run privileged production migrations on web startup. Test meaningful constraints against SQL Server. Keep connection strings private and health checks independent of SQL.

Validation: All 21 tests passed against a freshly migrated local SQL Server database, covering constraints, ownership, concurrency, and canonical audit hashes. The migration also applied to the local development database, the model matches its migration, and the dependency vulnerability scan is clean. CI repeats migration/model and SQL integration checks. Runtime permissions remain QUILS-10; durable claims/dispatch remain QUILS-14.

## QUILS-13: Add SES and local SMTP adapters with SNS feedback handling

Status: Implementation complete. Depends on completed QUILS-8 and QUILS-9. Local/simulated delivery only; hosted sending remains disabled.

Implement IEmailSender with SES v2 as default and SMTP/Mailpit locally, HTML/text Razor verification templates, and configurable sender/base URL. Templates render at dispatch; durable dispatch later assigns token validity. ACS remains a later optional adapter. Validate SNS signatures and confirmation destinations; auto-confirmation is an explicit disabled-by-default option. Handle feedback idempotently, including concurrent duplicates and reordered events. Suppress permanent bounces and complaints; transient bounces do not permanently block recipients. Reject suppressed deliveries. Test invalid/duplicate feedback, templates, provider requests, and suppression using local Mailpit and signed fixtures without live AWS mail. Keep SES sending disabled until subscription, durable dispatch, and approved production access are ready; AWS changes and real sends need owner approval.

Validation: All 98 tests passed against local SQL Server, Azurite, and Mailpit. Coverage includes rendered Razor HTML/text messages, SMTP capture, simulated SES requests, both SNS signature versions, invalid sources/signatures, certificate trust/cache/failures, concurrent duplicates, reordered feedback, suppression, rollback, and retryable outages. Build and dependency checks passed. No cloud configuration or live email changed; durable dispatch and sender verification remain subsequent work.

## QUILS-14: Implement durable event-driven work and email dispatch

Status: Implementation complete. Depends on completed QUILS-9 and QUILS-13. The owner approved the reviewed background-dispatch proposal; hosted email delivery remains disabled.

Record the wakeup/recovery design before worker implementation: SQL Server atomic leases, retry/backoff, lease expiry, crash recovery, provider ambiguity, and delivery semantics. Implement email dispatch with reusable work contracts and local tests for competing workers, duplicate wakeups, crashes, delayed mail, exhausted retries, and suppression. Start magic-link validity at actual dispatch and protect sensitive payloads. Idle workers must not poll SQL; maintenance is batched. Propose exact hosted wakeup resources, schedule, sizing, and cost for approval before deployment. Do not promise exactly-once SES delivery without supported deduplication, implicitly approve a queue, or enable public delivery.

Approved design: SQL remains authoritative; opaque queue wakeups launch a finite worker. Insert the SQL work row within a transaction, persist its unique Blob wakeup hint, then commit and publish. The worker uses a locking read to distinguish committed work from a rollback, including under snapshot defaults. Keep hints until work is terminal so a storage-only hourly recovery pass can republish missed wakeups without querying idle SQL. Leases, delayed wakeups, and bounded backoff cover retries; record submission before contacting the email provider and stop automatic retries after an ambiguous submission. Protect token-bearing payloads with persistent Data Protection and activate verification tokens at dispatch. Provide explicit worker/recovery commands and Azurite/SQL/Mailpit tests. Deploy only the approved queue, hint container, dispatch/recovery jobs, worker identity/access, and application configuration; keep actual deployment settings and operational details in local notes. Public sending, AWS changes, and later document-processing workers are outside this approval.

Validation: All 114 local SQL/Azurite/Mailpit tests passed, including key-ring outages, duplicate/competing claims, crash recovery, protected payload persistence, delayed tokens, suppression, retry exhaustion, and actual SMTP delivery. CLI/container checks, dependency audit, migration/model checks, Bicep, and secret scans passed. The approved hosted resources are deployed; direct worker/storage-only recovery checks and an automatically triggered synthetic wakeup succeeded. The web revision is Running/ready with the expected health response. No AWS configuration or live email changed.

## QUILS-15: Build sender email verification and envelope-scoped sessions

Status: Implementation complete. Depends on completed QUILS-9, QUILS-12, QUILS-13, and QUILS-14. Hosted SQL is connected; public email delivery remains disabled pending SES readiness.

Implement no-account sender email entry, magic-link verification, and a short-lived session for one new draft. Use 256-bit tokens, hashed auth-token storage, 15-minute validity from dispatch, and atomic single use scoped to email/envelope/purpose. Reject invalid, expired, replayed, concurrently consumed, and cross-envelope access. Add secure HttpOnly sessions, antiforgery, neutral request responses, configurable rate limits/daily caps, suppression, and creation/verification audit events. No accounts, passwords, or CAPTCHA. Browser notices use short-lived HttpOnly cookies and clean redirects; JSON clients receive bodies. Exercise the Mailpit-to-draft browser flow and failure cases. Public delivery remains gated on SES readiness.

Implementation defaults: a two-hour non-sliding sender session and five verification requests per address per UTC day, both configurable. Serialize durable address caps in SQL and keep HTTP rate limiting separate. Create the draft, initial audit event, and outbox/work atomically using the existing hint protocol; publish only after commit. The verification link consumes its hashed token and records verification atomically, then redirects to a clean draft URL. The UI clearly indicates when email delivery is unavailable. Preserve the existing branding and disclaimers; PDF upload is QUILS-16.

Validation: All 131 SQL/Azurite/Mailpit tests pass, including concurrent address caps and single-use verification, invalid token scope/state, transaction rollback, audit hashes, antiforgery, neutral JSON responses, one-time browser notices, rate limits, session isolation, tampering, expiry, and shared-key replica/restart behavior. The actual browser-to-Mailpit-to-draft flow passed on desktop and at 390-pixel phone width, including link replay, notice consumption, keyboard navigation, and logo rendering. The local app and listener ran together using the shared build. Build and dependency checks passed. Public delivery remains disabled; no AWS changes or real email were needed.

## QUILS-16: Build verified-sender PDF upload and preview confirmation

Status: Implementation complete. Depends on completed QUILS-9, QUILS-12, and QUILS-15. Hosted SQL is connected; public email delivery remains gated on SES readiness.

Let the verified sender upload/read/replace one PDF in their own draft, preview it with PDF.js, and explicitly confirm it. Enforce content sniffing, configurable byte/page limits, bounded streaming, safe filenames, and access isolation. Reject malformed, encrypted, XFA, and already-signed PDFs; this slice excludes images and Office conversion. Record original/normalized hashes, page dimensions/count, and upload/confirmation audit events. Replacement clears obsolete confirmation/incompatible draft state; failures must not leave falsely completed records. Confirming the preview does not send or freeze a signing copy. Exercise the responsive/accessible browser flow, restarts, isolation, invalid/oversize inputs, and storage failures using synthetic fixtures.

Implementation defaults: 10 MiB and 20 pages, configurable within the storage limit. Pin MIT-licensed PDFsharp 6.2.4 (later releases changed license) for validation and PDF rewriting; serve a pinned local PDF.js distribution for preview with page navigation, zoom, selectable text, and a download fallback. Bind preview/read/confirmation to the current document version, and use SQL concurrency checks so stale confirmations or competing replacements cannot overwrite newer state. Commit document references and audit events only after both private blobs exist; handle failed writes and replacement cleanup without deleting a committed document. Clear obsolete field/suggestion/confirmation state when replacing. No new cloud resources, Office conversion, sealing, or public email enablement.

Validation: All 156 tests passed, covering PDF parsing/rejection, encrypted and signed input, crop/rotation/user units, hashes, bounded streams, concurrent replacements, stale confirmation, scoped access, actual Razor upload/read/confirmation with antiforgery, and storage/database failures including uncertain commits. Desktop and 390-pixel browser checks exercised Mailpit verification, PDF.js page navigation/zoom/selectable text, confirmation, replacement, invalid/oversize input, notices, and restart persistence. Release build, dependency audit, model/migration check, vendor hash verification, and secret scans passed. PDF attribution is included in the published app; public email remains disabled.

## QUILS-18: Validate completion PDF sealing and timestamp feasibility

Status: Implementation complete. Independent feasibility spike; QUILS-16 continues separately.

Add a standalone .NET 10 developer harness using MIT-licensed PDFsharp 6.2.4 and permissive BouncyCastle. Generate synthetic PDFs and ephemeral certificates only. Keep the harness separate from production APIs, certificate providers, storage, and signing workflow.

Do not claim Acrobat/DSS or PAdES conformance without observed results. Generated PDFs and keys remain ignored or outside git. Validation: The Windows offline harness passes CMS/timestamp verification in .NET, BouncyCastle, and OpenSSL, rejecting changed/truncated/appended content, unexpected certificates, and HTTP/malformed/imprint/nonce TSA failures. It confirms two initial TSA calls and one on signer reuse. The built-in signer's unsigned signing-time is rejected by BouncyCastle; the custom prototype fixes attribute placement and adds signed certificate binding. [Findings and commands](pdf-seal-feasibility.md) document the observed `adbe.pkcs7.detached` container and pending Acrobat/DSS/public-trust/PAdES integration gates. Dedicated Windows/Linux CI repeats the harness. No runtime or cloud configuration changed.

## QUILS-22: Prototype PDF field stamping and verify geometry and text fidelity

Status: Implementation complete. Uses completed QUILS-20 geometry and QUILS-18 seal findings; final workflow integration follows this feasibility work and QUILS-21 preparation. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Create a standalone .NET 10 developer harness with MIT PDFsharp 6.2.4 and permissive font/rendering/parser dependencies. Stamp synthetic text/multiline text, full names, dates, true/false checkboxes and signature/initials image marks with aspect ratio preserved. Cover Letter, A4, landscape, all right-angle rotations, offset CropBox and non-default UserUnit pages.

Establish observed Unicode/script and fitting behavior. Reject unsupported glyphs, overflow, missing values, malformed/out-of-page geometry, too-small fields and changed source documents rather than silently corrupting output. Keep fixtures synthetic and generated PDFs/images ignored.

Document reproducible offline commands, Windows/Linux CI, the actual PDFsharp transform, font/sizing/image fitting behavior, limitations and production integration gates. No envelope mutations, production freezing/stamping, signature acceptance, mail, deployment or cloud changes. Pass appropriate checks and secret scans, commit/push/open/attach/merge a small QUILS-22 PR and move Done. Do not claim final PDF/signature validity beyond observed checks.

The prototype's native renderer/parser dependencies remain isolated from production.

PDF.js browser checks cover Letter, rotated crops/UserUnit 2, and unembedded Helvetica with system fonts disabled. The vendor check verifies all 200 files and pinned OFL fonts; app publish retains exact font/license hashes and interact.js notice. All 179 application tests pass. Findings, reproducible commands and production integration limits are documented in docs/pdf-field-feasibility.md.

## QUILS-24: Implement bounded sender-prefill PDF rendering for the send foundation

Status: Implementation complete. Builds on merged QUILS-22 and includes merged QUILS-23 review. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Implement a reusable production-managed sender-prefill renderer with MIT PDFsharp 6.2.4, the verified rotated CropBox/UserUnit transform, and embedded hash-pinned OFL Liberation Sans 2.1.5. Managed Apache-2.0 PdfPig may validate cmap glyphs; native PDFium/SkiaSharp/PDFtoImage stay isolated in testing. Initialize the PDFsharp font resolver safely for repeated/concurrent renders.

Accept bounded normalized PDF bytes with expected SHA-256 and validated page geometry, and only unassigned Text/Checkbox/Date fields with stable IDs, valid placement and nonempty valid values. Recheck existing encrypted/signed/XFA/malformed-source policy. Reject changed bytes/metadata, assigned/invalid fields, unsupported glyphs/scripts/controls, invalid dates/checkboxes, overflow/unreadable fields and byte/field limits. Preserve original content and physical font sizing, use NFC/explicit lines/deterministic 8â€“12 point fitting and vector checkboxes. Return bytes/hashes only after successful bounded serialization; never return partial output.

Embed the exact font and complete notice in build/publish. Exercise the actual production renderer with the independent nine-case Windows/Linux harness, preserving existing prototype cases; test rejection, bounds, repeated/concurrent rendering and no application mutation. Verify original embedded text, Unicode/fitting/checkbox output and publish hashes. Document limits and the later authorization/revalidation/immutable hash/outbox transaction contract. No public endpoint/UI, signer value/image stamping, envelope mutations, persistent blob writes, mail, deployment or cloud changes. Pass checks and secret scans, commit/push/open/attach/merge a small QUILS-24 PR, then move Done.

Malformed-source regression: PDFsharp throws a plain System.Exception from its parser when startxref is absent. Translate that exact upstream parser failure into the existing document-rejection path, preserve unrelated/runtime failures, and add an upload-validator regression case as part of this source-policy requirement. Rendering uses the existing storage byte limit with an additional 64 MiB in-memory ceiling, existing page cap, and the editor's maximum of 200 fields.

Validation: All 222 application tests pass, including preserved multi-page original text, NFC/Unicode, source/output hashes, invalid/assigned/duplicate/overflow fields, source policy and metadata changes, byte/page/field limits, eight concurrent renderer instances, and exact embedded-font/upstream-notice integrity. The independent harness passes all nine actual production-renderer cases alongside nine prototype cases and 25 negative cases on Windows and offline in the minimal Linux app runtime. Published output retains exact font/OFL/PdfPig license/notice bytes. The web dependency graph excludes native PDF test engines; dependency audit and vendor verification pass. No endpoint, persistence mutation, mail or cloud changes.

Building a snapshot writes no database/audit/token/blob state. Future consent displays verified stored text rather than re-rendering current settings, demonstrates access/retention, and records an affirmative act separately from adopting a signature.

Document conditional consumer duties, operator legal review and later integration. Test deterministic hashes, version/content/contact/envelope binding, missing-policy failure, Unicode/controls/limits, literal insertion, migration/model compatibility and no mutation.
