## QUILS-26: Authenticate sequential signers with single-use invitation sessions

Status: Implementation complete. Builds on merged QUILS-24 and QUILS-25. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Build internal SignerAccess token creation/redemption and separate short-lived, non-sliding signer cookie/context services. Tokens have 256-bit entropy, SHA-256-only storage and envelope/signer/purpose/email binding; plaintext exists only in memory for a future actual-dispatch caller. Start default 24-hour invitation expiry at dispatch, default signer session two hours, bound configuration and clamp to envelope expiry. Expose no issuance endpoint, consent/signing UI or mail operation.

Require a Sent, unexpired envelope, current eligible sequential signer, frozen PDF and verified stored ERSD snapshot. Reject premature, replayed, expired, wrong-purpose/party/email, signed/declined or foreign access without consuming a token. Under the shared envelope lock reread bindings and atomically consume with a signer-attributed link_opened audit event; create cookie identity only from committed persisted data. Bind claims to signer, envelope/public ID, email and frozen PDF/disclosure hashes, rejecting duplicate/mixed/wrong-scheme claims and changed bindings. Isolate sender/signers; later consent/PDF handlers receive only a scoped frozen-PDF read context.

Test concurrent redemption/replay, timing, state/order/scope isolation, changed document/disclosure and ERSD tampering, cookie scopes, audit rollback and actor evidence with synthetic data. Document future mail/consent integration, add safe config/examples, pass checks/secret scans, commit/push/open/attach/merge a small QUILS-26 PR and move Done. No signing enablement, consent acceptance, field mutation, live mail, deployment configuration or cloud changes.

Frozen reads use a distinct immutable object ID in the existing normalized container. Add narrowly scoped signer copy authorization for that exact envelope/object and live session; writes/deletion require sender identity. Future handlers must revalidate current signer context before copying. No new container or cloud resource.

Validation: All 262 application tests pass. New coverage verifies four concurrent consumers yield one identity/audit event, replay and premature order leave tokens untouched, purpose/party/email/state/expiry/ERSD/frozen-reference isolation, changed cookie/current bindings, non-sliding context expiry, audit rollback and retry, dispatch-time token expiry/clamping and entropy. Actual HTTP cookie tests verify HttpOnly/Lax, sender isolation, no refresh and middleware rejection at the expiry boundary. Real Azurite copy permits only the frozen object and denies signer writes/deletes, other kinds/objects/envelopes and mixed/expired/ambiguous claims. Model and vendor checks pass. No public endpoint/UI, consent, mail or cloud changes.

## QUILS-72: Enforce fixed demo retention on signer sessions and checkpoints

Status: Done. Merged as PR #76; native Jira Done. All 1,618 combined tests, 11 Node tests and ten exact-head CI checks passed.

Enforce the verified fixed upload-retention cutoff throughout public signer access and owned checkpoint confirmation/downloads, building on merged QUILS-69 guard, QUILS-62/63 policy and QUILS-68 signing cap. This is independent of QUILS-70 image persistence and queued QUILS-71 public intake. Preserve their branches and plan entries. Use a separate QUILS branch; create matching plan before implementation and assign Codex service-account status workflow.

Authorize the invitation/session/party scope before private retention reads. Bound new or continuing public signer authority by the earlier verified retention cutoff without extending original token/session/envelope lifetimes. Reject access at the exact UTC cutoff even for legacy enrolled requests whose stored signing/session expiry is later, or accepted/processed own-checkpoint flows intentionally available after review/signing expiry. Verify the current immutable policy/provenance; missing or tampered enrolled evidence cannot fall back to ordinary authority. Ordinary and historical unenrolled behavior stays explicit and compatible.

Apply the shared guard at appropriate public invitation/session, document/asset, consent/adoption/field/review/Finish and own-checkpoint boundaries rather than granting raw storage authority. Recheck after slow buffered storage/hash reads and at mutation commit boundaries so no new expired result or Finish is confirmed. Preserve exact source/adoption/field/nonce/session/candidate/hash checks, CSRF and rate limits, no-store/no-referrer, private bounded proofs/cookies and clean redirects. An accepted signer cannot regain editing. Already-started downloads and party-held copies cannot be recalled; no packet-level revocation claim.

Do not rewrite historical sent/disclosure/consent/Finish/expiry evidence, silently enroll or backfill legacy records, or mutate policy/work state on GET. Trusted internal worker/historical verification must remain available through its existing narrow system capabilities without impersonating a signer. This slice adds no purge or worker-publication behavior, completed-recipient lookup integration, sender metadata gates, hosted enablement, cloud resources/permissions, live email/TSA, purchases, dependencies, fabricated operator policies, OTP or deferred AI.

Test meaningful actual SQL/Azurite and HTTP cases for exact cutoff before/after invitation redemption and active/accepted sessions, earlier lifetimes, legacy enrolled later-expiry contexts, wrong party, missing/tampered policy, expiry during storage, cancellation/recovery, Finish boundary rejection, unchanged audit/work state and ordinary compatibility. Verify internal accepted-checkpoint/history worker capabilities still work after original signer-session expiry while public reads remain bounded by retention. Exercise real synthetic desktop/mobile/keyboard signer PDF and own-checkpoint flows before/at cutoff, with truthful error/recovery and no real agreement or outbound mail. Run relevant combined regression/model/vendor/license/secrets, commit/push/open/attach/pass exact-head checks/merge and native Jira Done.

Implementation design: preserve the exact existing VerifiedSigner identity, session ID and serialized session hash for legacy consent/adoption/Finish bindings. Carry a separate internal verified retention deadline on SignerReadContext, and use its effective temporal limit for new receipts/previews/review candidates and final commit checks. Only a newly redeemed session is shortened directly. Apply the policy only after complete party/token/context authorization; active and accepted-own-checkpoint paths share the guard. New invitation eligibility may stop after cutoff, while existing trusted checkpoint/history worker capabilities remain unchanged. A signer-only exception filter returns private generic 410 for an authenticated expired retention scope and 503 for unverifiable retention evidence; ordinary unknown/expired identity behavior stays unchanged. Add post-audit temporal checks to redemption, consent/decline, adoption and Finish as needed, without rewriting prior evidence.

Validation: 16 new actual SQL/Azurite/HTTP cases pass, along with 46 existing access/consent/cookie cases. Real synthetic desktop/mobile/keyboard validation completed consent, typed adoption, separate signature/text application, immutable PDF review, affirmative Finish and worker checkpoint publication/download. At the exact fixed cutoff, the same still-live legacy session receives the generic unavailable response for confirmation, direct checkpoint PDF and signer metadata; the 390-pixel mobile view has no horizontal overflow. No real agreement, external mail or timestamp request was used. Full combined regression passed all 1,618 tests, and all ten exact-head CI checks passed before merge.

## QUILS-27: Build signer disclosure consent, frozen PDF review and decline flow

Status: Implementation complete. Depends on merged QUILS-25 and QUILS-26. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Add a safe invitation landing with non-consuming GET, rate-limited antiforgery POST redemption, committed signer cookie and clean redirect. Use generic invalid/expired responses, no-store/no-referrer and safe notices; keep tokens out of logs. Every signer page, disclosure and frozen-PDF read revalidates current sequential scope. Display encoded exact stored ERSD and title/contact, retainable downloads and local PDF.js preview/navigation/zoom with accessible fallback. Bound/stage and verify PDF bytes before successful responses and show failures honestly.

Issue a short-lived protected access receipt bound to signer/session/PDF/ERSD after verified server PDF preparation. Require separate unchecked agreement and access/retention confirmations. Record the user's reported preview/download method and honest receipt meaning; do not claim proof of human reading or automatic legal sufficiency. Under the envelope lock revalidate context/receipt and atomically save ConsentAt/IP/UA plus signer-attributed version/hash/access evidence. Reject failed access/missing/expired/foreign receipts and prevent duplicate consent evidence. Continue to read-only document review; adoption/filling/Finish and sender send/issuance are later work.

Offer Decline with optional bounded reason before consent/signing. Atomically decline signer/envelope, audit actor/reason, prepare durable sender notification and publish work after commit. Add the needed notification dispatch/template with retry/idempotence and no SQL polling. End other sessions through current-state checks, show truthful confirmation and stored paper/contact instructions, and preserve the distinction between decline and the broader withdrawal procedure.

Test actual Razor HTTP scopes/CSRF/prefetch/rate limits, PDF/ERSD hash/storage failures, receipt/affirmative boundaries, concurrent/idempotent consent, decline/audit/outbox rollback/retry and unchanged fields/PDF. Exercise desktop, 390-pixel phone and keyboard browser flows using clearly synthetic non-agreement fixtures and Mailpit. Pass required checks/scans, commit/push/open/attach/merge a small QUILS-27 PR, then move Done. Do not fabricate operator policies or enable live mail, deployed settings or cloud resources.

Decline remains available with work/email disabled: retain its queued notification and use an explicit operator command to republish a specific item after approved configuration is available, without idle SQL scanning. Recheck frozen PDF availability on consent POST. Optional decline reasons use the existing 512-character schema bound.

Validation: 289 application tests pass, including actual Razor CSRF/private scopes/rate limiting, exact downloads, missing/malformed/oversized/hash-mismatched PDF storage, failure between page load and consent, receipt/affirmation boundaries, concurrent consent, audit/outbox rollback and retry, disabled-work preservation and explicit recovery, commit-before-publication, no-token decline dispatch, duplicate wakeups and local Mailpit delivery. No signature, field/PDF mutation, live email, deployed configuration or cloud changes.

Render Razor HTML/text SignerInvite with document title, sender, optional message, signer name/role and safe invitation landing URL. ## QUILS-29: Validate and normalize bounded signature PNG assets

Status: Implementation complete. Independent asset-processing foundation for the agreed v1 adoption flow, building on merged PDF-image feasibility and consent work. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Add a pure managed PNG normalizer for signature/initials assets. Input is browser-normalized static 8-bit RGB/RGBA PNG; later adoption UI previews the normalized output and binds its hash, normalizing supported upload formats in the browser first. This slice grants no storage access and adds no endpoint, adoption action, field filling or Finish. Use existing permissive dependencies/.NET only; no native runtime or paid service.

Bound encoded input to 512 KiB and dimensions to 2048 by 1024. Validate PNG signature, IHDR, chunk lengths/order/CRCs, contiguous IDAT and final IEND/no trailing data. Bound chunk count and decompression before raster allocation. Reject unsupported bit depth/color modes, interlace/APNG, unknown critical chunks, malformed/truncated/excess rows and invalid filters; safely implement all five filters. Require visibly nonblank ink on a white preview background, without identity/handwriting/biometric claims. Crop surrounding whitespace with modest transparent padding, preserve the visible mark/aspect, encode a simple static RGBA PNG with fresh CRCs and no ancillary metadata, and return owned bytes/hash/dimensions/normalization version.

Test RGB/RGBA, every filter, split IDAT, blank/transparent/colored/alpha pixels, crop/padding/aspect, metadata stripping, hash/ownership, CRC/order/truncation/trailing data, animation/interlace/modes, compressed bombs, size mismatches and all caps. Independently verify outputs with PDFsharp's managed image reader and the existing image-stamping harness where useful, including Linux. Keep PDFsharp MIT 6.2.4, document the adoption boundary, pass checks/scans, commit/push/open/attach/pass CI/merge a small QUILS-29 PR and move Done. No cloud, deployed configuration or live mail changes.

The normalized output also stays within 512 KiB, and padding stays within dimension caps. Reject palette/color-key transparency inputs rather than silently changing transparency; later browser conversion produces the previewed sRGB RGB/RGBA pixels before normalization.

Validation: All 379 application tests pass, including 58 focused PNG cases. Independent PDFsharp embedding and PdfPig sample extraction verify normalized pixels. The field-stamping harness uses production-normalized signature/initials assets and passes all nine prototype/nine sender-renderer geometries, 25 negative checks and naive-transform detection on Windows and an offline minimal Linux container. Output preserves the visible mark/alpha while stripping ancillary metadata, stays within caps, and returned bytes/hashes are owned. No new dependency, native production runtime, storage access, endpoint, consent/adoption/Finish action or cloud changes.

## QUILS-32: Record deliberate signer field application bound to current adopted marks

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-30 and QUILS-31. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Build the backend for deliberate signer-owned field application on QUILS-30/31 adoption. No public field UI, Finish, Signed status, PDF mutation or mail in this slice. Include field/type, frozen PDF/disclosure hashes and current adoption/image hash for mark fields. Concurrent/repeated exact applications record once; stale different values or stale/replaced adoption IDs are rejected. An audit/storage failure must leave the field unchanged and retryable. Current state remains editable before Finish; required-field/completion enforcement follows in the review/Finish story.

Test SQL Server/Azurite consent/session/current sequential scope, foreign/unassigned fields, forged blob/adoption refs, revision conflicts/concurrency/idempotence, exact mark hashes, current asset reads after preview expiry, replacement/stale application and explicit reapplication, typed/manual/server-derived values, clearing and rollback/audit retry. ## QUILS-34: Render signer-applied fields into hash-bound current PDF copies

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-32 and QUILS-22/24; independent of the QUILS-33 field UI. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Build the bounded internal PDF renderer needed by v1 Finish/current-copy processing. Render one signer's server-saved fields from exact hash-pinned PDF bytes into a new bounded owned copy/SHA-256 with provenance. Preserve sender-prefill/prior signer contents, CropBox offsets, rotation and UserUnit; never mutate input/frozen storage/records. Reject foreign/unassigned/duplicate/invalid fields, unsupported geometry/types, unsaved or stale required entries and incomplete mark bindings. Stamp only explicitly applied current signature/initials fields; independently verify adoption ID, field image SHA-256, exact canonical normalized PNG bytes/version and binding. Empty optional fields remain blank. Full Name derives from the server signer record; Date Signed derives from a server-supplied Finish instant in UTC, rejecting client overrides. Generic ISO calendar fields remain distinct. Preserve bounded Unicode/line fitting and mark aspect/alpha; fail closed on overflow/unsupported content.

The later Finish transaction owns authorization, consent/session/sequential checks, rechecks/audit/state/storage publication and the server Finish instant. Renderer output is not a completed signature or seal. Sequential copies must start from the prior exact current copy while the original sent PDF stays immutable. No invented disclosure/retention policy or scope expansion.

Verify input immutability and preservation of prior content/geometry through sequential rendering. ## QUILS-35: Prepare immutable signer review candidates bound to current saved fields

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-32/34; independent of the QUILS-33 UI. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Build the internal review-candidate backend for v1 review/Finish, with no public endpoint/UI, Finish/Signed transition, field application, signer progression, mail, seal or cloud change. Explicit preparation verifies current sequential signer/session nonce/consent, frozen or current PDF/ERSD, exact adopted assets, owned field revisions, all required entries except deferred Date Signed and a deliberately applied current signature. Render only locked server records, then stage a fresh immutable bounded PDF/object ID and candidate record with source/candidate/recipe/snapshot hashes and envelope/signer/adoption/session/preparation/expiry binding. Original source bytes, fields and signing status remain unchanged.

Candidate Date Signed is prospective: render the server UTC day and expire at the minimum of ten minutes, session/envelope expiry and next UTC midnight. The later explicit Finish must verify its actual server date matches the reviewed date and consume the receipt atomically; no signing time is asserted during preparation. Full Name and saved entries remain server-derived/exact.

Protect receipts against foreign request/signer/nonce/source/ERSD/adoption/snapshot/revision state. Reuse a live exact candidate for unchanged preparation; invalidate changed snapshots and bound active/lifetime counts. Reads never mutate/refresh expiry. Reject expired/foreign/used/missing/tampered candidates or assets. Preserve cookie storage permissions with narrow exact-object/operation/time internal PDF capabilities; verify bounded complete length/hash before exposure and recheck live context after storage. Record preparation evidence atomically without claiming user review/signing. Known rollbacks clean up; unknown commits retain possibly referenced bytes for idempotent recovery. No values/pixels/receipts in logs or URLs.

Add EF composite ownership/adoption constraints, retention ordering and runtime SQL grants for the new table without deploying resources. Test SQL Server/Azurite scope/nonce/consent/sequential isolation, required/current bindings, exact staged hashes/bytes, explicit idempotence, snapshot/replacement staleness, UTC rollover/session expiry, limits, malformed/foreign receipts, read-only retrieval and storage/audit/commit cleanup/recovery. Run appropriate .NET/model/vendor/license/secret checks, commit/push/open/attach/check/merge a small PR and move Jira Done. Actual operator disclosure policies remain empty.

Validation: All 579 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Fifty new SQL Server/Azurite cases cover concurrent exact reuse, source/nonce/consent/sequential scope, required saved fields, snapshot/revision/replacement invalidation, prospective UTC date and midnight/session/envelope expiry, exact candidate bytes/hashes, malformed/consumed receipts, limits, evidence/asset/PDF corruption, storage/audit rollback, failed cleanup and lost commit recovery. Database ownership/immutability/retention ordering and runtime column grants pass. Model/vendor/license and secret checks pass. No public endpoint/UI, Finish/Signed/progression, field/source mutation, mail, seal, cloud or dependency change. See docs/signer-review-candidates.md.

## QUILS-36: Accept explicit Finish against the exact reviewed PDF and freeze signer entries

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-35; independent of QUILS-33 browser validation. Owned by the overnight-development chat.

Implement the internal explicit Finish acceptance transaction. No public endpoint/UI, Signed transition, sequential progression, invitations, mail, seal or cloud change. A dependent worker slice publishes the checkpoint and records completion. Require affirmative Finish intent and the canonical versioned statement, live isolated signer session/nonce/request/consent/sequential scope and the exact immutable review receipt. Under the common envelope lock, recheck source/ERSD/adoption/owned saved-field revisions, required entries, exact candidate PDF and preparation evidence. Choose actual Finish time only on the server; require the UTC date to match the reviewed prospective date and receipt/session/envelope limits to remain live after storage. Never rerender different bytes after acceptance or accept client values/pixels/object refs/timestamps.

Atomically consume the candidate, create one immutable envelope/signer-owned durable Finish acceptance record with candidate/source/recipe/snapshot/disclosure/adoption/session hashes, server intent time and statement version, fill Date Signed from that actual server UTC date and append signer-attributed finish-accepted evidence. Preserve exact candidate bytes for the later checkpoint worker; do not queue an unsupported handler or claim processing is complete. Signer status stays pre-completion until verified checkpoint publication records signer_completed.

Freeze field edits, consent decline, preview/adoption/replacement and fresh invitations for the accepted signer while processing is pending. Exact retries of the same protected accepted receipt/session return the same durable acceptance once, including after receipt expiry while the isolated session remains live. Different receipt/nonce/request/signer state cannot claim it or mutate it. Validation/storage/audit failures leave fields editable and receipt retryable; lost commit acknowledgment recovers accepted state/evidence once and preserves candidate bytes.

Add EF ownership/candidate uniqueness, immutable-record/runtime permission guards and retention/processing documentation. Test SQL Server/Azurite explicit intent/version, scope/nonce/required/current snapshot/date, exact-byte corruption, concurrency/idempotence, post-Finish field/decline/adoption/invitation denial, Date Signed time, storage-crossed expiry, rollback and uncertain commit recovery. Keep sent PDF immutable and PDFsharp MIT 6.2.4. No optional OTP expansion, fabricated operator policies, live email, purchases or cloud changes. Validate model/vendor/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done.

Validation: All 621 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Forty-two new SQL Server/Azurite cases cover affirmative/versioned intent, exact concurrent acceptance, nonce/request/consent/sequential/required snapshot scope, actual Date Signed and frozen entries, UTC rollover and storage-crossed expiry, corrupt candidate/source/assets, old-receipt rejection, expired-session denial, audit rollback, lost commit recovery, immutable replay evidence and database ownership/retention order. Model/vendor/license and staged/full-history secret checks pass. No public endpoint/UI, Signed/progression/work-handler/mail/seal/cloud or dependency change. See docs/signer-finish.md.

## QUILS-37: Publish accepted signer checkpoints and advance the verified sequential current copy

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-36. Owned by the overnight-development chat. Internal backend only; no public review/Finish/confirmation UI, final completion/seal/certificate/delivery, live email, cloud changes or fabricated policies.

Atomically enqueue one supported Rendering checkpoint work item with Finish acceptance, using the existing durable event-driven work/lease/retry/recovery framework and best-effort wake hints after commit. No idle SQL polling or duplicate work on accepted replay. Preserve actual Finish time and exact reviewed bytes: rendering occurred before acceptance, so the worker verifies/publishes that same immutable candidate rather than generating an unreviewed PDF.

Under envelope-first locks and a still-owned work lease, validate immutable Finish/candidate/source/ERSD/adoption/field snapshot bindings and preparation/acceptance evidence. Verify bounded complete source/candidate bytes through short-lived exact-object read-only worker capabilities independent of the original signer's expired session. Recheck lease/live envelope/current-source state after storage. Missing/tampered/expired/voided/out-of-order state cannot become Signed or invite the next signer. Unknown commit retries recover one checkpoint/evidence.

Atomically publish a versioned envelope current-copy reference to the exact accepted PDF/hash, mark that signer Signed using actual accepted Finish time/IP/UA, set acceptance ProcessedAt and append signer_completed with checkpoint_sha256 plus source/acceptance/review/snapshot/recipe provenance. Keep SentPdf bytes/reference/hash immutable and signer entries frozen. Verify current-copy ownership/order/hash-chain provenance; never fall back to SentPdf when an earlier completed signer's checkpoint is missing or inconsistent.

Bind later invitation dispatch/redemption, consent/PDF reads, capture/adoption, fields and review to that verified current copy. Queue exactly one next sequential invitation through the existing durable writer in the same publication transaction, with correct PDF/ERSD binding and token activation only at dispatch; publish hints after commit. Last signer publication leaves finalization/sealing to a later supported slice without queuing an unimplemented handler or claiming envelope completion.

Add narrow accepted/completed-signer confirmation and own-checkpoint reads for a live original isolated session and exact accepted proof after Signed/current-copy changes, preserving request/signer/nonce/source/disclosure binding. Do not restore editable context, expose other envelopes or broaden ordinary signer cookie storage permissions. Accepted proof survives review expiry; session/envelope termination gates reads.

Add migration/constraints/permissions and retention/current-reference deletion ordering. Test SQL Server/Azurite two-signer progression end to end: exact review bytes/earlier contents/server dates, immutable sent source, correct later invitation/consent/adoption/review copy, stale original sessions, own-checkpoint reads and once-only invitation. Cover concurrency/lease/idempotence, source/field/evidence/PDF corruption, worker capability bounds, storage/audit/queue/commit failure/recovery, suppression/void/expiry and last-signer pending finalization. Keep permissive dependencies and PDFsharp MIT 6.2.4; no purchases, optional OTP or scope expansion. Validate model/vendor/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done.

Validation: All 676 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Fifty-five new SQL Server/Azurite cases cover the real two-signer queue/dispatch/redemption/consent/adoption/field/review/checkpoint pipeline, exact PDF bytes/earlier contents, actual accepted times, immutable sent source and linked audit evidence. Tests also cover expired signer sessions, scope/nonce/capability isolation, required/current state and prior-copy failure, oversized/corrupt/missing objects and evidence, leases, suppression, rollback, disabled work/delivery, hint/queue failures, unknown commits and once-only recovery after later progress. Synthetic Mailpit invitation delivery passes. Model/runtime permissions/vendor/license and secret checks pass. Legacy unbound invitation records fail closed instead of rebinding. No public endpoint/UI, final completion/seal, live email, cloud or dependency change. See docs/signer-checkpoints.md.

## QUILS-38: Review exact signer PDFs, Finish deliberately, and download owned checkpoints

Status: Done. Depends on merged QUILS-33 and QUILS-37. Owned by the overnight-development chat. Public signer review/Finish/confirmation UI only; no public Send, final sealing/completion/delivery, cloud changes, live email or fabricated operator policies.

Extend saved-entry review with explicit CSRF-protected/rate-limited POST preparation of an immutable candidate after required/current saved entries and adoption are valid. GETs do not prepare candidates or mutate fields/signing state. Show the exact candidate PDF with the saved owned-entry summary, prospective server Date Signed, expiry and corrective links. Carry bounded protected candidate proof only in a short-lived HttpOnly/SameSite cookie or private POST body, never a URL/log. Changed fields/adoption/source/nonce/expiry/UTC date require new preparation; never fall back to SentPdf or autoapply marks.

Authorize and verify complete exact candidate PDF bytes/hash before preview/download. Issue separate protected access proof bound to candidate/PDF/session nonce/expiry only after preparation for that response; describe it as server preparation and client-reported access, not proof the person read it. Support keyboard/download-only access and PDF.js preview. Finish requires that matching access proof, affirmative review/intent acknowledgment, canonical Finish statement/version and immutable review receipt. Existing backend chooses actual server time and atomically accepts/freezes/queues checkpoint work; accept no client values/pixels/object refs/timestamps or bulk applications.

Redirect accepted or replayed Finish to a clean confirmation URL, retaining protected accepted proof only within the original live isolated session/envelope lifetime. Explain pending checkpoint processing and use manual refresh or event-driven notification without recurring SQL-backed polling. Published own-checkpoint downloads use the narrow QUILS-37 contract after review expiry while the original session is live; later progress must not change that owned copy. Do not claim final envelope sealing/completion. Frozen signers cannot return to editable capture/fields/review or create a new signature; confirmation does not restore edit authority.

Use clean redirects/short-lived HttpOnly once-only notices and JSON bodies, CSRF/isolated authorization, bounded private cookies, no-referrer/no-store headers and sane rate limits. Preserve acknowledgment/drafts on recoverable validation/storage/audit/queue errors where relevant. Unknown response/commit retries show the same acceptance/work once. Mixed/sender/foreign/stale proof exposes no other signer/envelope values.

Test TestServer/SQL Server/Azurite read-only GETs, explicit preparation, exact PDF/access-proof gating, missing/forged/expired/stale/foreign/nonce proofs, actual Date Signed, canonical statement/intent, cookies/redirects/CSRF/rate limits, storage/audit/hint/commit recovery, pending/manual refresh/published own downloads, once-only Finish and frozen edit paths. Exercise real desktop/mobile/keyboard synthetic non-agreement flows, changed entries/replacement, expiry/error recovery and processing-to-download. Keep permissive dependencies/PDFsharp MIT 6.2.4, secret scan, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done. No optional OTP, purchases or scope expansion.

Validation: All 724 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Twenty-six new TestServer/SQL Server/Azurite cases cover explicit preparation, exact PDF/access-proof gating, CSRF, intent/statement checks, foreign/stale/expired/nonce proofs, actual Date Signed, private cookies/clean redirects/notices, no GET signing mutation, pending/published own downloads, frozen edit routes, session/envelope termination, throttling, storage/audit/hint/work-disabled failures and unknown-commit once-only recovery. Submitted confirmations survive rolled-back recoverable failures when the candidate remains verifiable. Proofless or malformed PDF/confirmation requests fail before opening SQL. Real desktop/mobile/keyboard synthetic flows passed preparation, both PDF pages, changed-entry and replacement invalidation/reapplication, injected missing-asset recovery, deliberate Finish, pending/manual refresh and owned download. Reviewed and published downloads were byte-identical, including after the review deadline expired while the original session remained live. Final sealing/completion, sender Send and actual operator policy values remain separate. Merged as PR #41 after all checks passed; see docs/signer-review-finish.md. Public home and health checks also pass with database/storage/work unconfigured; accepted-state recovery resolves checkpoint services lazily only after a valid protected signer proof.

## QUILS-39: Expose reviewed Send and private sender confirmation

Status: Done (PR #42). Depends on merged QUILS-28 and QUILS-38. Owned by this chat.

Expose the agreed v1 sender Send action and a private in-session confirmation/status screen, using the merged QUILS-28 atomic send/freeze backend and QUILS-38 signer flow. This is a small public sender UI slice; final sealing/completion delivery and sender confirmation email/status magic links remain subsequent slices. Do not fabricate actual operator disclosure policies, enable live email, create/change cloud resources or incur purchases.

Extend the existing preparation review with an explicit antiforgery-protected, rate-limited Send POST. Require the current verified sender session, valid current reviewed preparation/source/field/signer/recipient state, recorded excluded-document and sender authorization acknowledgments, and complete configured ERSD/operator policy requirements. Reuse the existing server-validated review and Send contract; stale or missing review, changed fields/source/party ordering, invalid policies, disabled durable work/delivery or revoked/expired sender scope fail closed with truthful corrective guidance. GETs never send, freeze, queue invitations or sign. Never accept client PDF bytes, hashes, storage references or timestamps as authority.

On accepted or unchanged idempotent replay, redirect to a clean private sender confirmation URL with a short-lived HttpOnly once-only notice; JSON clients receive a body. Describe a frozen sent request and queued first invitation accurately, without claiming delivery or final signing. Offer read-only status within the original verified envelope-scoped sender session, showing only that sender's request, ordered signer status, accepted/pending checkpoint distinctions where relevant and completion/decline/expiry state. Access must continue to be envelope/email/session scoped and live; it must not restore draft editing after Send or broaden signer/foreign/sender-mixed access. Any status reads are user initiated, with no recurring SQL polling. No anonymous status URL or new persistent credentials.

Preserve exact immutable sent PDF/source hashes and once-only existing Send/audit/invitation semantics. Recover unknown commit/response outcomes to the same sent result; validation/storage/audit/queue errors do not falsely report delivery or reset the draft. Use no-store/no-referrer, request bounds, CSRF/rate limits, safe encoded labels/values and private receipts/body/cookies, never action notices or protected proofs in URLs/logs. Keep actual operator policy values empty until owner supplied; synthetic test policies must be explicitly non-agreement fixtures.

Before implementation keep this native Jira story and docs/plan.md matched and use a separate QUILS-numbered branch/worktree. Test TestServer/SQL Server/Azurite current-review Send, scope/CSRF/stale/acknowledgment/policy gates, immutable source and once-only invitation/audit, failure/unknown-commit recovery, clean redirect/notices/JSON, frozen draft and private status access. Exercise real desktop/mobile/keyboard synthetic sender preparation through Send confirmation and first invitation redemption into the merged signer flow. Run meaningful checks, permissive dependency/license and secret scans, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and mark native Jira Done. Keep MIT PDFsharp 6.2.4; no optional OTP or scope expansion.

Clarification: require a deliberate confirmation to send the reviewed request to the listed signers, separately from excluded-record suitability. Read-only status may describe a retained expired, declined or voided sent request while the verified sender session remains live; this grants no signing/editing/write authority. Bind exact Send replay evidence to the reviewed envelope version so an unknown commit recovers once without accepting a different review. Sender confirmation email and status magic links remain subsequent work.

Validation: All 752 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Twenty-eight new cases cover public Send through invitation redemption, consent/adoption/field application, exact review/Finish, checkpoint publication and the next invitation; CSRF/scope/current review/intent/policy/expiry gates, immutable once-only Send replay, JSON/private notices/headers, frozen preparation, status limits and storage/audit/hint/unknown-commit recovery. Rejection, bounce, complaint and uncertain submission are distinguished from queued/submitted status. Real desktop, 390-pixel and keyboard synthetic flows passed reviewed Send, once-only confirmation/manual refresh, local Mailpit invitation redemption, two-page consent and review, deliberate adoption/application/Finish, pending-to-published sender status and owned download. Reviewed and published PDFs were byte-identical. A second browser tab changed the draft: stale Send was rejected, acknowledgments were preserved and explicit reload was required. Actual empty operator policies disabled Send; an injected owned synthetic delivery rejection showed corrective status. No live email/cloud/dependency/final-completion change. See docs/sender-send.md; PR #42.

## QUILS-40: Add the internal timestamped platform PDF seal engine

Status: Done. Merged as PR #43. Depends on merged QUILS-18. Owned by this chat.

Implement the internal production PDF seal engine from merged QUILS-18 feasibility, pinned to PDFsharp 6.2.4 (MIT), as a small prerequisite for final completion. This slice signs an already prepared, hash-verified PDF once and returns bounded sealed bytes plus verified seal/timestamp metadata. It does not prepare completion certificates, mutate envelope state, persist final blobs, queue unsupported jobs, expose public sealing/verification endpoints or send mail.

Introduce a narrow pluggable certificate contract and a configured PFX-file adapter with private configuration only. Validate certificate/private-key capability, validity and suitable signing usage; fail closed on configured but unusable material. Persistent Data Protection-encrypted self-signed fallback and the Key Vault adapter remain explicit subsequent provider work, not an ephemeral production fallback. Do not contact Azure/AWS or provision resources.

Require an explicitly configured timestamp service and never fall back to an untimestamped seal. Use bounded, cancellable RFC 3161 transport, HTTPS without redirects in production and loopback HTTP only for synthetic Development tests. Send only a SHA-256 signature imprint with a fresh unpredictable nonce. Reject bad HTTP/content type/length/status, malformed responses, wrong imprint/algorithm/nonce, stale/future time, invalid token signature/EKU/certificate binding and untrusted TSA certificate chains. Trust uses OS roots or explicitly configured private root material; tests use a generated synthetic TSA. Document revocation/LTV limitations without claiming PAdES, AdES/QES, qualified timestamps or Adobe trust.

Use a per-operation PDFsharp custom signer with bounded signature reservation so sizing does not contact a live TSA. Produce one detached CMS platform signature with SHA-256 and an RFC 3161 signature timestamp. Validate the resulting complete PDF ByteRange, detached CMS signature/digest, certificate binding and embedded timestamp before returning it; reject malformed, already signed, hash-mismatched, encrypted or oversized input and incomplete/trailing-uncovered output. Keep source bytes unchanged and record the actual output hash separately. Do not turn signer marks into signer-held cryptographic signatures.

Test meaningful production sealing and independent validation using generated local certificates/TSA only: correct exact source and page preservation, detached signature/timestamp, tampering/ByteRange/trailing data, invalid PFX/key/usage/validity, nonce/imprint/EKU/chain/time failures, network limits/cancellation/redirects and missing configuration. Render synthetic output to verify its existing contents/geometry remain correct. Reuse existing permissive dependencies where available; add only a pinned permissive dependency if necessary. Keep secrets, certificates/private keys, TSA/operator values and test artifacts ignored. Run relevant full tests/model/vendor/license/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small QUILS-numbered PR and native Jira Done. No live TSA use, real mail, cloud changes, purchases, fabricated operator policies or scope expansion.

Validation: All 805 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Fifty-three new production cases cover exact source hashes, complete PDF ByteRange/Contents and detached CMS verification, signed certificate/time binding, nonce/imprint/algorithm/EKU/key/chain/time checks, RSA/ECDSA and concurrent isolated operations, bounded PFX/transport/output/capacity, redirects/cookie isolation, cancellation/recovery, unprepared/already-signed input and tampered/truncated/trailing/padding output. OpenSSL independently verifies production CMS and timestamp output and rejects changed content/imprints. Poppler renders both source/sealed pages pixel-identically; all four rotations preserve crop/media boxes, UserUnit and text. Model, browser-asset/license and dependency vulnerability checks pass. Actual PFX/TSA values remain empty; no public endpoint, envelope completion, final persistence, mail, live TSA or cloud resource change. See docs/platform-pdf-seals.md.

## QUILS-41: Add persistent demo and Key Vault seal certificate providers

Status: Done. Merged as PR #44. Depends on merged QUILS-40 (PR #43). Owned by this chat.

Complete the agreed pluggable seal-certificate providers on top of QUILS-40. Add explicit/automatic selection of configured PFX, Key Vault certificate backing secret or persistent self-signed demo material. Automatic selection must reject ambiguous/partial configuration; a configured unusable provider must never silently fall back to a fresh identity. Preserve operation-owned private-key handles and the existing certificate validity/usage/key checks. No public endpoint, envelope completion, final packet publication or email.

Persist the demo fallback as a write-once Data Protection-encrypted PKCS#12 object in the existing protected keys storage boundary, separate from document retention. Generate RSA-3072 with the QuillSign Demo Seal subject and document-signing purpose only when an explicit sealing operation first requires missing fallback material. Bootstrap/startup remains usable without certificate/network/storage access. Concurrent instances converge on one persisted identity; restarts reuse it. Bounded reads, cancellation and conditional creation must fail closed on corruption, wrong protection keys, missing/unavailable persistent storage or expired/unsuitable existing material. Never overwrite/rotate a saved identity automatically or persist plaintext private keys. Do not create hosted containers/resources.

Implement the Key Vault adapter through its selected certificate's exact backing secret/version, validating configured vault/name, returned identifiers, enabled state and public certificate/private material equality. Use managed identity only, lazy bounded/cancellable calls and no secret bodies/resource identifiers in logs. Key Vault values stay in ignored configuration and examples remain empty. Tests use generated certificates, fake SDK transport/credentials and local Azurite only; never inspect/contact a live vault or Azure identity endpoint.

Document selection, private configuration, encrypted persistence/retention, expected self-signed trust limitations and owner-managed rotation/permissions. Keep this Jira story and docs/plan.md matched before implementation. Use a separate QUILS-numbered branch/worktree and start implementation only after QUILS-40 merges. Test PFX/Key Vault/demo selection and no-fallback failures, exact secret/certificate binding, SDK scope/enabled/format/size/cancellation failures, stable encrypted fallback across instances/restarts, concurrent first-use creation, tampering/wrong keys/storage failure/expiry and actual production sealing with the retained identity against a generated local TSA. Preserve permissive dependencies and PDFsharp MIT 6.2.4; validate full meaningful tests/model/vendor/license/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done. No Azure/AWS resource changes, live cloud/TSA/email calls, purchases, fabricated operator policies or expanded v1 scope.

Validation: All 867 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass. Sixty-two new cases cover provider selection/no fallback, engine resolution without storage I/O, write-once encrypted RSA-3072 identity under concurrent first use, separate Data Protection service reconstruction from an Azurite key ring, unknown-create recovery, tampering/wrong wrapping keys/expiry/invalid saved profiles and cancellation. Real Key Vault SDK JSON and pipelines use fake transports/credentials to verify exact backing version, scope, enabled/validity/format/private-certificate binding, response limits/deadlines and caller cancellation; private byte arrays are cleared on success/failure/cancellation. Both new providers produce production PDF seals verified against a generated local TSA. The 53 production seal cases and independent OpenSSL positive/tampered-content/imprint checks pass. Model, vendor/license distribution, vulnerability and secret checks pass. PDFsharp remains MIT 6.2.4; new pinned Azure SDK packages retain MIT notices. No live vault/identity/TSA/email call, hosted container creation, cloud change, completion publication or public endpoint.

## QUILS-42: Render appended and standalone completion certificates

Status: Done. Merged as PR #45. Depends on merged QUILS-37 and QUILS-40; final publication will also use QUILS-41. Owned by this chat.

Add the internal completion-certificate PDF renderer required by the agreed v1 final packet. This is a pure rendering prerequisite: accept a bounded immutable evidence snapshot supplied by the future authorized completion service, render appended certificate pages and the separately prepared standalone certificate, and preserve the exact current checkpoint's visible pages/geometry when assembling the unsealed packet. No public endpoint, database state transition, final blob publication, seal/TSA call or email in this slice.

Define a typed snapshot for envelope/document identifiers and page counts; original/converted/sent/current-checkpoint hashes; sender and verified email-link procedure; ordered signer names/emails/roles, recorded viewed/consent/actual Finish/checkpoint times, ERSD version/hash, accepted mark images and their hashes, IP/user-agent evidence; completion recipients; and the complete ordered audit chronology with sequence/hash linkage. Render truthful stored evidence only. Missing optional names/client-local time/TSA friendly names are explicitly not recorded rather than inferred. Audit rendering lists every event and its recorded actor/time/hash plus approved evidence fields; never render raw arbitrary audit JSON, bearer links/tokens, session/review proofs or mail transport internals. Mask the address in the authentication-procedure description while retaining the party addresses in the party list. This renderer does not itself establish that a caller's snapshot is authoritative; future durable completion must derive and verify it from stored accepted evidence and the full checkpoint/audit chain.

Use MigraDoc/PDFsharp pinned at permissively licensed 6.2.4 for pagination, readable headers/footers, repeating table headers and bounded long-text wrapping. Use existing approved branding/assets and embedded permissive fonts where supported. Treat titles/names/roles/user agents as plain untrusted text; validate counts, field lengths, SHA-256 shapes, signer order/times and bounded normalized PNG image bytes/dimensions/hash bindings before allocating/rendering. Reject incomplete mandatory evidence and oversized output; never silently truncate the event list or images. Keep source pages unchanged in appearance, with exact geometry, and clearly separate completion pages. Include a DEMO / synthetic warning when the snapshot explicitly represents that mode.

Avoid circular hashes: the appended pages identify the source/current checkpoint and pre-seal audit anchor, and direct readers to the embedded platform signature and standalone certificate for the executed-file hash/seal/timestamp details. The standalone certificate is generated after the executed PDF is sealed and includes that exact executed-file SHA-256 and verified seal/TSA metadata. It must not claim to contain its own final sealed hash. Shared core evidence remains identical across both forms; neither form claims individual signer-held certificates, public CA/Adobe trust, qualified signing or PAdES conformance.

Create/update native Jira and docs/plan.md before implementation, use a separate QUILS-numbered branch/worktree, preserve other work and keep PDFsharp at MIT 6.2.4 with permissive dependency/license notices. Test real multi-page pagination, long/Unicode/plain-text values, complete chronology, signature/initial images and hash failures, malformed/oversized evidence, circular-hash separation, exact source geometry/text preservation and output bounds/cancellation. Render representative synthetic output with Poppler and visually inspect every page, with no private data/certificates committed. Run meaningful full/model/vendor/license/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge a small PR and native Jira Done. No Azure/AWS/resource changes, live cloud/TSA/email, purchases, fabricated operator policies or scope expansion.

Validation: All 907 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass with no build warnings. Forty new cases cover both production-sealed certificate forms against a generated loopback TSA; shared-evidence/final-hash separation; complete chronology and recorded consent/Finish/checkpoint times; plain Unicode/control/markup-like text; exact PNG/hash binding; twenty long signer rows with repeating headers and glyph bounds; tall mark proportions; source geometry/text on all four rotations; malformed/stale/oversized evidence, output limits and cancellation. Poppler renders both source pages and all six appendix pages pixel-identically inside the sealed eight-page packet. Every page of the source, six-page appendix and six-page standalone certificate was visually inspected with readable branding, marks, hashes and chronology. Model, browser-asset/MIT notice distribution, vulnerability and secret checks pass. MigraDoc/PDFsharp remain MIT 6.2.4. This pure renderer has no public endpoint, authoritative evidence collector, durable completion/publication, mail, live TSA/cloud call or fabricated operator policy.

## QUILS-43: Verify and assemble authoritative completion evidence snapshots

Status: Done. Merged as PR #46. Depends on merged QUILS-37/41/42 (PR #45). Owned by this chat.

Add the internal read-only authoritative completion-evidence collector required before durable final packet preparation/publication. It supplies QUILS-42 with an owned immutable snapshot and the exact last verified signer checkpoint. This slice does not expose HTTP endpoints, prepare unsupported jobs, seal PDFs, publish final blobs, mark Completed or send email. It depends on merged QUILS-37/41 and QUILS-42 (PR #45).

Require a sent, frozen envelope with every sequential signer genuinely checkpoint-published as Signed, backed by its accepted Finish and exact reviewed candidate/field/adoption/source/disclosure bindings. Verify the complete canonical audit chain and immutable sent/current-copy chain without fallback. Derive sender/party/recipient/document metadata from frozen recorded state, verify original/converted/sent/current hashes and exact source references, and reject partial, missing, tampered or cross-envelope evidence. Capture a stable server snapshot time and the complete audit prefix through its anchor; later seal/publication/delivery events necessarily remain external to that pre-seal certificate snapshot.

Project only real persisted evidence: sender verification, each signer's recorded one-time-link redemption/view/consent/actual Finish/checkpoint-publication times, the correct ERSD version/hash, accepted names/roles/addresses, exact accepted applied signature/initial assets and hashes, recorded Finish IP/user agent, recipients and every audit event's sequence/time/actor/IP/user agent/hash linkage. Verify semantic acceptance/consent/adoption/field bindings rather than trusting Signed flags or recalculating fresh marks. Never use a later current adoption, re-render signer checkpoints, infer missing local time/name/authentication facts, print raw audit JSON or expose tokens, proofs, session identifiers and transport internals. Preserve the distinction between actual Finish time and later publication time.

Use narrow internal completion-read capabilities for exact envelope/object/kind/source/asset bindings after verifying eligibility; do not impersonate a sender/signer, revive expired sessions or grant edits. The collector must work after original signing sessions expire when Finish was accepted on time, while rejecting unaccepted/late Finish, declined/voided/incomplete states. Bounded and cancellable blob reads verify the recorded hashes; original/sent/checkpoint/accepted mark bytes stay immutable. Recheck frozen state/audit anchor after asynchronous I/O and reject a concurrent change rather than returning a mixed snapshot. No broad document, listing or keys-container permissions.

Exercise after-session/envelope-time passage for already timely accepted work, forged Signed flags, missing acceptance/consent/redemption/audit rows, broken chain/current copy, field/adoption/source/disclosure/metadata tampering, cross-envelope capabilities, missing/corrupt/oversized storage, concurrent changes and cancellation without writes or authority restoration. No Azure/AWS/resource changes, live cloud/TSA/email, purchases, fabricated operator policies, optional OTP or scope expansion.

Validate the latest pre-Send signer-list and recipient snapshots from canonical preparation audit records rather than trusting mutable rows. ## QUILS-44: Persist immutable completion candidates for crash-safe final sealing

Status: Done. Merged as PR #47. Depends on merged QUILS-43 (PR #46). Owned by this chat.

Add internal durable, immutable completion-candidate preparation after QUILS-43. Preserve the exact verified last signer checkpoint and freeze the authoritative typed evidence snapshot, its audit-prefix anchor and deterministic recipe/hash bindings. Render and persist the unsigned executed packet (exact source pages plus completion appendix) once for later sealing. Store only bounded versioned evidence needed to reproduce/verify the completion certificate, exact source identity/hash/geometry and candidate blob/hash/page-count metadata. Do not expose an HTTP endpoint, invoke a live or test TSA from production preparation, publish final sealed objects, set Completed, send mail or enqueue an unsupported job.

Preparation is explicitly invoked by a future supported completion workflow, idempotent and safe across retries/concurrent workers/restarts. Recheck eligibility and source/frozen accepted evidence at the durable commit boundary after rendering/storage I/O. Reject incomplete/declined/voided/changed sources, party/field/adoption/acceptance bindings and audit-prefix tampering. Preserve the verified historical pre-seal snapshot while allowing only verified append-only operational audit events, including its own preparation event. Do not accidentally invalidate the candidate because its own database/audit writes changed optimistic row versions. Never revive signer or sender sessions or edits. Original, sent and reviewed checkpoint PDFs remain immutable.

Use a dedicated narrow internal candidate storage capability, exact envelope/object/kind bindings, bounded/cancellable reads/writes, conditional creation and exact-byte verification. No broad document/key/listing authority. Candidate publication must fail closed on missing/corrupt blobs or conflicting existing rows/objects. Resolve uncertain storage/SQL commit outcomes by re-reading authoritative state and exact existing bytes, never deleting possibly committed/referenced objects; rollback cleanup follows existing supported deletion/recovery mechanisms. Persist stable ownership and hash bindings so future final sealing can reuse the same prepared bytes and evidence across crashes without recapturing a later snapshot.

Create/update this native story and docs/plan.md before implementation, start after QUILS-43 merges and use a separate QUILS-numbered branch/worktree. Test a genuine two-signer send/consent/adoption/apply/review/Finish/checkpoint/collect/render/prepare chain, repeated and concurrent preparation, service reconstruction, exact source/evidence/packet/page bindings, no mutation of original/reviewed copies, verified audit tail vs prefix/state changes, storage corruption/size/scope failures, cancellation, rollback and uncertain commit recovery without completion/mail side effects. Validate migrations/full meaningful tests, real PDF appearance when output changes, model/vendor/license/vulnerability/secret checks; commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge and native Jira Done. Keep permissive dependencies and PDFsharp/MigraDoc MIT 6.2.4. No Azure/AWS/resource changes, live cloud/TSA/email, purchases, fabricated operator policies, optional OTP or expanded v1 scope.

Integration clarification: use a distinct candidate object kind within the existing private normalized-document container, accessible only through an exact expiring internal operation capability. Keep the collector's full optimistic binding for new preparation commits and add a separate frozen-row binding that excludes only the audit anchor, so verified append-only audit tails do not invalidate a saved candidate. Revalidate the stored snapshot against the authoritative collector's same historical prefix and recorded snapshot time, including exact current mark bytes, rather than merely trusting stored JSON/hash columns. Commit candidate metadata and a canonical system preparation audit event atomically under the envelope lock. On a known pre-commit rollback, delete only the attempt's proven unreferenced object using its narrow capability; never enqueue an unsupported purge handler. After a possible SQL commit, retain uncertain objects and recover by authoritative re-read/retry, including exact storage verification. A crash before candidate commit has frozen no snapshot; a committed candidate is never regenerated or replaced.

Database integration: grant the runtime role SELECT and INSERT on completion candidates, explicitly deny UPDATE/DELETE, and verify these restrictions through the existing operator-invoked runtime verifier. Exercise genuine preparation/read under that restricted role, with no hosted identity/resource changes.

Thirty-seven new candidate cases exercise genuine two-signer preparation, one immutable saved snapshot/packet across retries and service reconstruction, historical sessions/deadlines, concurrent winner reuse and losing-object cleanup, lost upload/SQL commit acknowledgements, unresolved commit/cancellation retention, audit rollback, source/state/audit changes during I/O, missing or rehashed preparation evidence, corrupt/missing/oversized storage, metadata tampering and exact expiring operation/identity/path scopes. A real restricted runtime SQL user successfully prepares/reads the candidate while UPDATE/DELETE remain denied; existing runtime verification and fresh migration/model checks pass. All thirteen persisted synthetic packet pages were rendered and visually inspected, with both source pages pixel-identical to the accepted checkpoint. Run full/model/vendor/license/vulnerability/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge and native Jira Done. Preserve permissive dependencies and PDFsharp/MigraDoc MIT 6.2.4.


Integration clarification: add an explicit CompletionAccess token purpose and dedicated nullable publication ID, publication-recipe SHA-256 and frozen-party SHA-256 bindings, with a matching migration/purpose/scope/hash constraints; do not reinterpret signer-document columns. Derive exact sender/signer/recipient identities from the verified frozen publication under the common envelope lock. Issue only for those parties through explicit trusted backend operations, persist only the canonical 256-bit token hash, and support a caller-owned transaction preparation path for later protected delivery payloads. Use configurable bounded technical defaults (24-hour link, one-hour readonly session), always shortened by completion retention; old signing expiry does not revoke completed-only retrieval. Redemption takes the envelope lock before the token, validates canonical issuance/source/publication/party evidence, conditionally consumes once, appends its audit and returns a fresh dedicated readonly session identity only after a confirmed commit. Bind the session to the consumed token/revision, party/publication/recipe, redemption time, nonce and original session expiry. Unknown commits return no session; consumed links must not be silently replayed into another session, and later explicit fresh issuance provides safe recovery. Keep dedicated purpose/scheme parsing separate from sender/signing authority; no public endpoint, cookie login flow or mail producer in this backend slice. Recheck consumed-token/session/retention/frozen source after actual final-pair I/O. Keep both final files and all prior signing evidence unchanged.

Integrity handling clarification: normalize conflicts in the verified completed-source projection as unavailable/integrity failures across the shared published-pair reader and completed-download guards, so the new readonly backend and existing completed-session downloads fail closed with controlled recovery. Preserve active-draft conflict semantics. A dedicated-scheme principal must match a bounded Data Protection session receipt, and every read rechecks consumed-token revision/nonce/evidence and frozen publication/party state. Standalone issuance/redemption owns a clean unit of work and clears rolled-back pending entities before retry; caller-owned transaction preparation leaves rollback ownership with its caller.

Validation clarification: seed deterministic midnight test time before consent and field fixture creation, rather than rewinding a live-date fixture. Preserve production chronological acceptance checks and verify accepted retry across UTC midnight independent of the wall-clock calendar.

Validation: Current-head CI passes all 1080 .NET tests and all applicable checks, including production PDF seals on Windows/Linux. Local checks pass 47 focused backend/persistence/permission cases, 92 review/Finish regression checks and 11 Node tests. Genuine frozen sender/signers/recipient access remains readonly after signing expiry and private-provider unavailability; exact-byte reads, once-only redemption, publication/token revisions, retention, malformed/scope/expiry, rollback/unknown-commit and post-I/O failures are covered. Real synthetic mobile/native-keyboard downloads match published bytes, and concurrent completed-source changes produce controlled private errors without new grants, files or seals. Model, pinned vendor/MIT distributions, vulnerability and staged/full-history secret checks pass. No public redemption/login or completion mail in this backend slice, and no live cloud/TSA/email calls, purchases or fabricated operator policies.

## QUILS-50: Expose safe completed-record retrieval and downloads

Status: Done. Merged as PR #53. Depends on merged QUILS-49 (PR #52). Owned by this chat.

Depends on merged QUILS-49. Add the public readonly completion-link flow for sender, signers and completion recipients, using its dedicated verified session and exact published-pair backend. This is completed-record access only; preserve all original signing/edit authority boundaries, Finish/Date Signed and immutable files.

Use scanner-safe link entry: prefer an initial fragment/code input so raw one-time tokens do not enter HTTP request URLs/logs, remove the browser fragment promptly, and provide an explicit manual-code fallback. An antiforgery-protected, rate-limited Begin POST can establish a bounded private pending proof and clean redirect without consuming the token or mutating SQL. Ordinary GETs never redeem links, prepare files, append domain state or grant a login. A separate explicit redemption POST validates the pending proof/nonce and antiforgery before calling the once-only backend. Competing tabs/pending links cannot silently change the link the user is opening. Keep raw code/protected values out of rendered notices, URLs, request logs and replayable action query strings.

Only a confirmed redemption establishes the dedicated named authentication cookie and bounded private session receipt, with original backend expiry and no sliding renewal. Named-scheme authentication/ticket expiry and the protected receipt must both pass current token/party/publication/retention checks. Never create sender/signing cookies or revive edits. Expired/consumed/invalid links and uncertain commits receive truthful private recovery guidance without party/envelope enumeration or automatic reissuance.

Provide an authenticated private completed-record page with clearly distinguished executed PDF and standalone certificate actions, current completion/retention facts and clean manual refresh. Explicit antiforgery/rate-limited download preparation verifies actual stored files and current authorization, records verified preparation without claiming receipt/saving/delivery, and issues a bounded exact-kind/session/nonce/publication/hash/version-bound file grant. Readonly clean file GETs recheck after I/O and cannot create grants, regenerate or replace PDFs. Generalize or reuse the existing grant module carefully so new completion-reader authority cannot cross into original sender/signer audiences, and all existing session downloads remain intact. Proofs remain in bounded private HttpOnly cookies/body, never file URLs; replay remains readonly within its original short scope/expiry, with no false global one-use claim.

Exercise real synthetic desktop/mobile/native-keyboard fragment/manual-code entry, scanner-safe GET behavior, explicit redemption, readonly page and both exact downloads after original signing expiry. Test CSRF/rate, private clean redirects/history/headers, competing-tab nonce conflicts, invalid/consumed/expired/wrong-purpose/wrong-session/audience/proofs, token and source/publication changes, lost/unknown commit recovery, missing/corrupt storage and explicit retry, no restored edits or GET SQL mutation. Compare actual downloaded bytes with verified publication and preserve original own checkpoints. Run full/model/vendor/license/vulnerability/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass checks/merge and native Jira Done.

Completion attachments/outbox/adapters, actual email delivery and independent lookup/resend remain later supported slices. Do not enable live email, create/change cloud/resources/immutability policies, call live TSA, purchase services, fabricate operator disclosure/retention policies, add optional OTP or expand v1 scope. Keep permissive dependencies and PDFsharp/MigraDoc MIT 6.2.4.

Integration clarification: use /complete/access with fragment/manual-code entry, a ten-minute private pending proof containing the canonical code and fresh nonce, and a separate explicit nonce-matched redemption POST. Begin must not query or mutate SQL; raw fragment/code is cleared from browser history and not echoed. Confirmed redemption creates the dedicated named authentication ticket with the backend's original expiry and a separate bounded private receipt cookie under the completed request path, preserving the backend's strict seven-claim principal contract. Use /complete/<publicId> for the readonly page. Distinguish completion-reader audience from original Sender/Signer party roles in shared file grants, route/cookie paths and named authentication; a reader may itself be a sender, signer or recipient but must not cross audiences. Keep full authenticated-cookie digest binding through CookieManager, receipt binding, current ticket/retention bounds and post-I/O backend authorization. Add the cookie handler without changing default sender authentication or liveness SQL behavior; ordinary GETs remain non-mutating.

Validation: All 1092 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass, with no build warnings and all 14 exact-head CI checks green, including Windows/Linux production PDF seals. Twelve new HTTP/entry/failure cases cover scanner-safe SQL-free GET/Begin, explicit CSRF/nonce-bound once-only redemption, every frozen party after original signing expiry, renamed/chunked named cookies, bounded readonly replay, cross-audience/kind/session/proof denial, expiry, storage/source/token changes and rollback/unknown-commit recovery. Real synthetic desktop/mobile/native-keyboard flows pass fragment/manual-code entry, same-tab fragment changes, explicit opening, exact downloads of both final files, two-tab stale confirmation rejection and storage outage with clean explicit recovery. All downloaded bytes match the verified 13-page executed PDF and 11-page certificate; all 24 pages were rendered and inspected. Recovery preserves two original files and two seals, without falsely claiming receipt, saving or delivery. ## QUILS-51: Support bounded PDF attachments in email adapters

Status: Done. Merged as PR #54. Depends on merged QUILS-50 (PR #53). Owned by this chat.

After merged QUILS-50, add the transport foundation for the agreed v1 completion email: a retainable fully executed PDF and standalone certificate attached to each party's own message. This slice changes the existing email abstraction and supported local SMTP/SES v2 adapters; it does not create a completion-mail producer, enqueue an unsupported mail kind, send live email, or change cloud resources.

Represent attachments as bounded immutable/copy-safe application/pdf data with safe filenames and explicit byte counts. Enforce conservative configured aggregate/message limits before provider submission; reject unsupported content, malformed/header-injection filenames, invalid byte data and oversize data without silent attachment omission or fallback. Preserve existing text/HTML verification and signer invitation messages, recipient isolation, suppression, provider failure classification, cancellation and ambiguous-submission behavior. Keep private PDF bytes, addresses, transport secrets and proofs out of logs.

Use the installed permissive MIME/SDK dependencies and verified current provider contracts; do not add GPL/AGPL dependencies or change PDFsharp/MigraDoc MIT 6.2.4. Transport exact supplied verified bytes without rerendering/resealing or weakening retention/access checks. Later completion dispatch must obtain the exact published pair and scoped backup link through supported workers; those producer/template/outbox/dispatch integrations remain a separate dependent slice.

Test actual local Mailpit multipart capture, decoded attachment byte/hash equality, deterministic safe content disposition/MIME and plain/HTML alternatives, no-attachment backward compatibility, cancellation, suppression, malformed/oversize/length failures before provider calls, and simulated SES v2 request payloads plus definite/ambiguous failures. No live AWS SES/TSA/email or purchases. Preserve the other chat's infrastructure and email approval work; current compact snapshot confirms it is inactive awaiting external approval.

Keep matching native Jira and docs/plan.md current before implementation, move In Progress when starting after QUILS-50 merges, use a separate QUILS-numbered branch/worktree, run meaningful regression/model/dependency/license/secret checks, commit/push/create/attach/pass exact-head checks/merge and native Jira Done in the same pass. No fabricated operator policies, optional OTP, lookup/resend, live sending, resources or scope expansion.

Transport design clarification: the existing pinned AWS SDK supports SES v2 Simple.Attachments, so retain Simple text/HTML and add application/pdf attachment objects with ATTACHMENT disposition and BASE64 MIME transfer encoding; pass raw byte streams and let the SDK encode API transport. SMTP uses existing MimeKit multipart attachments with the same exact bytes and explicit Base64. Use a copy-safe PDF attachment object with a derived byte length and non-writable reads, safe bounded .pdf filenames and at most two distinct files. No caller-supplied length or MIME type can contradict the copied content. Validate/snapshot before database/provider work and again at direct adapter entry. Conservative configurable defaults cap aggregate decoded attachment data at 20 MiB (hard 25 MiB) and the estimated encoded message at 32 MiB, including Base64 line wrapping, body/header allowance; reject rather than omit or send above the bound. These are technical transport bounds, not operator/legal retention policy. Keep already-canceled operations out of provider submission and preserve ambiguous failures after submission. No new attachment producer or queued mail kind in this slice. Official SES attachment and installed SDK contracts are verified; no dependency update or live AWS call is needed.

Requirements alignment: requirements.md L6 permits a link-only completion email with an explicit sender warning when attachments exceed provider limits. This transport slice must never silently drop files or choose fallback itself; the later verified completion producer owns that explicit warned fallback, including honest configured-limit guidance. Keep that later behavior available rather than treating attachment rejection as a permanent ban on completion notification.

Validation: All 1124 .NET tests and 11 Node tests pass with no build warnings and all 10 applicable exact-head CI checks green. Thirty-two new attachment/configuration cases cover copied non-writable bytes and list snapshots, safe Unicode filenames, PDF/type/count/size limits before database/provider work, configured hard bounds, unchanged no-attachment alternatives, exact SES raw payloads and stream disposal, definite/ambiguous failures including cancellation, and disabled/suppressed gates. Actual local Mailpit multipart capture decodes both PDF attachments byte-for-byte with exact filenames, PDF content types and Base64 disposition; plain-text/HTML alternatives and envelope metadata remain intact. Model, pinned vendor/MIT distributions, vulnerability and staged/full-history secret checks pass. No dependency upgrades, completion producer, live cloud/email/TSA calls, resource changes, purchases or fabricated operator policies.
